Tassattho passatha bho ayaṃ ambaṭṭho māṇavo ācariyakule avusitavā asikkhito appassutova samāno. Vusitamānīti ‘‘ahaṃ vusitavā sikkhito bahussuto’’ti attānaṃ maññati. Etassa hi evaṃ pharusavacanasamudācāre kāraṇaṃ kimaññatra avusitattāti ācariyakule asaṃvuddhā asikkhitā appassutāyeva hi evaṃ vadantīti.
263.

Here, when walking, a brāhmaṇa may gladly converse with a teacher-brāhmaṇa in the three postures.

But this youth, due to arrogance, while engaging in conversation, [thinks,] "I will apply all four postures," thus he said, "Indeed, Master Gotama, while lying down, [I spoke] while lying down."

Then, the Blessed One [said] to him: "Ambaṭṭha, conversation with a teacher is obtained among all teachers, whether walking with one who is walking, standing with one who is standing, or sitting with one who is sitting.

But you, while lying down, spoke with your teacher while lying down. What, is your teacher ox-shaped, or are you?"

He, becoming angry, said, "Those shavelings, Gotama,..." and so on.

There, it is fitting to say, "shaven in regard to the shaven, a śramaṇa in regard to the śramaṇa."

But this one, disparaging [them], says, "shavelings, little śramaṇas."

"Ibbha" means householders.

"Kāṇhā" means black, dark is the meaning.

Regarding "bandhupādāpacca," here "bandhu" is understood as Brahmā.

For brāhmaṇas call him "pitāmaha."

"Pādānaṃ apaccā pādāpaccā," the meaning is, "born from the back-foot of Brahmā."

The view of that one is this: brāhmaṇas issued from the mouth of Brahmā, kṣatriyas from the chest, vaiśyas from the navel, śūdras from the knee, śramaṇas from the back-foot.

And while speaking thus, he speaks without any regulation.

Then, he says, "I speak only to the Blessed One."

Then, the Blessed One [said]: "This Ambaṭṭha, having begun to converse with me from the time he arrived, relies only on pride in his speaking, like one seizing a venomous snake by the neck, like one embracing a mass of fire, like one touching a rutting elephant's trunk with his hand; he does not know his own measure.

Come, I will make him know," thinking thus, he said, "Atthikavato kho pana te, Ambaṭṭha," and so on.

There, the meaning is that the thing to be done is known as a task; having that is "atthika," [that is] the mind of that youth.

Having a need is "atthikavā," the meaning is, "your coming here, Ambaṭṭha, was for a purpose."

"Kho pana" is merely a particle.

"Yāyeva kho panatthāyā" means, "by whatever purpose."

"Āgaccheyyātha" means, "you might come to my presence or to the presence of others at any time."

"Tameva atthaṃ" this was said merely in terms of the masculine gender.

"Manasi kareyyātha" means, "you might do it in your mind."

This was said: "You were sent by your teacher for your own task, not for the sake of disparaging us; therefore, do that task in your mind."

Thus, having shown the conduct of those who have come to the presence of others, to suppress pride, he said, "Avusitavāyeva kho pana," and so on.

The meaning of that is, "Look, sirs, this Ambaṭṭha youth, being one who has not lived in a teacher's family, is uneducated, unlearned."

"Vusitamānī" means, "He thinks of himself, 'I have lived [there], I am educated, very learned.'"

Indeed, the cause of his harsh speech and conduct is none other than his not having lived [there]; indeed, it is because they have not grown up in a teacher's family that they speak thus, being uneducated, unlearned.
264
264. Atha kho ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhagavatā avusitavādena vuccamāno kupito anattamano bhagavantaṃyeva khuṃsento bhagavantaṃyeva vambhento bhagavantaṃyeva upavadamāno – ‘‘samaṇo ca me, bho, gotamo pāpito bhavissatī’’ti bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘caṇḍā, bho gotama, sakyajāti; pharusā, bho gotama, sakyajāti; lahusā, bho gotama, sakyajāti; bhassā, bho gotama, sakyajāti; ibbhā santā ibbhā samānā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti, na brāhmaṇe garuṃ karonti garukaronti (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.), na brāhmaṇe mānenti, na brāhmaṇe pūjenti, na brāhmaṇe apacāyanti. Tayidaṃ, bho gotama, nacchannaṃ, tayidaṃ nappatirūpaṃ, yadime sakyā ibbhā santā ibbhā samānā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti, na brāhmaṇe garuṃ karonti, na brāhmaṇe mānenti, na brāhmaṇe pūjenti, na brāhmaṇe apacāyantī’’ti. Itiha ambaṭṭho māṇavo idaṃ paṭhamaṃ sakyesu ibbhavādaṃ nipātesi.
264. Then Ambaṭṭha, the student, being addressed by the Blessed One with the accusation of being untrained, became angry and displeased, and he rebuked the Blessed One, reviled the Blessed One, disparaged the Blessed One, thinking, "The ascetic Gotama will bear me ill will." He said this to the Blessed One: "The Śākyan clan, reverend Gotama, is fierce; the Śākyan clan, reverend Gotama, is rough; the Śākyan clan, reverend Gotama, is impulsive; the Śākyan clan, reverend Gotama, is abusive. Though being of base origin ( ibbhā santā ibbhā samānā ), behaving as if they are base, they do not honor ( na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti ) Brahmins, they do not respect ( na brāhmaṇe garuṃ karonti ) Brahmins, they do not esteem ( na brāhmaṇe mānenti ) Brahmins, they do not revere ( na brāhmaṇe pūjenti ) Brahmins, they do not pay homage ( na brāhmaṇe apacāyanti ) to Brahmins. This, reverend Gotama, is not proper ( nacchannaṃ ), this is unseemly ( nappatirūpaṃ ), that these Śākyans, being of base origin, behaving as if they are base, do not honor Brahmins, do not respect Brahmins, do not esteem Brahmins, do not revere Brahmins, do not pay homage to Brahmins."

Thus, indeed, Ambaṭṭha, the student, made this first accusation of baseness ( ibbhavādaṃ ) against the Śākyans.
264.Kupitoti kuddho. Anattamanoti asakamano, kiṃ pana bhagavā tassa kujjhanabhāvaṃ ñatvā evamāha udāhu añatvāti? Ñatvā āhāti. Kasmā ñatvā āhāti? Tassa mānanimmadanatthaṃ. Bhagavā hi aññāsi – ‘‘ayaṃ mayā evaṃ vutte kujjhitvā mama ñātake akkosissati. Athassāhaṃ yathā nāma kusalo bhisakko dosaṃ uggiletvā nīharati, evameva gottena gottaṃ, kulāpadesena kulāpadesaṃ , uṭṭhāpetvā bhavaggappamāṇena viya uṭṭhitaṃ mānaddhajaṃ mūle chetvā nipātessāmī’’ti. Khuṃsentoti ghaṭṭento. Vambhentoti hīḷento. Pāpito bhavissatīti caṇḍabhāvādidosaṃ pāpito bhavissati. Caṇḍāti mānanissitakodhayuttā. Pharusāti kharā. Lahusāti lahukā. Appakeneva tussanti vā dussanti vā udakapiṭṭhe alābukaṭāhaṃ viya appakeneva uplavanti. Bhassāti bahubhāṇino. Sakyānaṃ mukhe vivaṭe aññassa vacanokāso natthīti adhippāyeneva vadati. Samānāti idaṃ santāti purimapadassa vevacanaṃ. Na sakkarontīti na brāhmaṇānaṃ sundarenākārena karonti. Na garuṃ karontīti brāhmaṇesu gāravaṃ na karonti. Na mānentīti na manena piyāyanti. Na pūjentīti mālādīhi nesaṃ pūjaṃ na karonti. Na apacāyantīti abhivādanādīhi nesaṃ apacitikammaṃ nīcavuttiṃ na dassenti tayidanti taṃ idaṃ. Yadime sakyāti yaṃ ime sakyā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti…pe… na apacāyanti, taṃ tesaṃ asakkārakaraṇādi sabbaṃ na yuttaṃ, nānulomanti attho.
264.

Kupitoti means kuddho: being angry.

Anattamanoti means asakamano: being displeased. Why, when the Blessed One knew his state of anger, did he speak in this way, or did he not know? He spoke knowing.

Why did he speak knowing? In order to subdue his pride.

The Blessed One, indeed, understood: "When I have spoken thus, this person, being angry, will insult my relatives.

Then I, just as a skilled physician expels and removes the provoked doṣa (fault), so too, with lineage against lineage, with designation of family against designation of family, having raised up the banner of pride that has arisen like the height of existence (bhavaggappamāṇena), having cut it off at the root, I will strike it down."

Khuṃsentoti means ghaṭṭento: colliding, shaking.

Vambhentoti means hīḷento: despising.

Pāpito bhavissatīti means caṇḍabhāvādidosaṃ pāpito bhavissati: he will be brought to a state of fierceness.

Caṇḍāti means mānanissitakodhayuttā: fierce, endowed with anger dependent on pride.

Pharusāti means kharā: rough, harsh.

Lahusāti means lahukā: light.

Appakeneva tussanti vā dussanti vā udakapiṭṭhe alābukaṭāhaṃ viya appakeneva uplavanti: they are pleased or displeased by little, just as a gourd shell floats on the surface of the water with very little.

Bhassāti means bahubhāṇino: speaking much.

Sakyānaṃ mukhe vivaṭe aññassa vacanokāso natthīti adhippāyeneva vadati: He speaks with the intention that there is no opportunity for another's word when the mouths of the Śākyas are open.

Samānāti idaṃ santāti purimapadassa vevacanaṃ: Samānāti means "this is," a synonym of the preceding word.

Na sakkarontīti na brāhmaṇānaṃ sundarenākārena karonti: Na sakkarontīti means they do not show honor to the brāhmaṇas in a beautiful manner.

Na garuṃ karontīti brāhmaṇesu gāravaṃ na karonti: Na garuṃ karontīti means they do not show respect to the brāhmaṇas.

Na mānentīti na manena piyāyanti: Na mānentīti means they do not endear with respect.

Na pūjentīti mālādīhi nesaṃ pūjaṃ na karonti: Na pūjentīti means they do not make an offering to them with garlands, etc.

Na apacāyantīti abhivādanādīhi nesaṃ apacitikammaṃ nīcavuttiṃ na dassenti tayidanti taṃ idaṃ: Na apacāyantīti means they do not show a respectful act, a humble demeanor, with salutations, etc. This is that.

Yadime sakyāti yaṃ ime sakyā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti…pe… na apacāyanti, taṃ tesaṃ asakkārakaraṇādi sabbaṃ na yuttaṃ, nānulomanti attho: Because these Śākyas do not honor the brāhmaṇas...and do not show respect, all of that, such as their not showing honor, is not fitting, not in accordance with, is the meaning.
265
265. ‘‘Kiṃ pana te, ambaṭṭha, sakyā aparaddhu’’nti? ‘‘Ekamidāhaṃ, bho gotama, samayaṃ ācariyassa brāhmaṇassa pokkharasātissa kenacideva karaṇīyena kapilavatthuṃ agamāsiṃ. Yena sakyānaṃ sandhāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃ. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā sakyā ceva sakyakumārā ca sandhāgāre santhāgāre (sī. pī.) uccesu āsanesu nisinnā honti aññamaññaṃ aṅgulipatodakehi aṅgulipatodakena (pī.) sañjagghantā saṃkīḷantā, aññadatthu mamaññeva maññe anujagghantā, na maṃ koci āsanenapi nimantesi. Tayidaṃ, bho gotama, nacchannaṃ, tayidaṃ nappatirūpaṃ, yadime sakyā ibbhā santā ibbhā samānā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti, na brāhmaṇe garuṃ karonti, na brāhmaṇe mānenti, na brāhmaṇe pūjenti, na brāhmaṇe apacāyantī’’ti. Itiha ambaṭṭho māṇavo idaṃ dutiyaṃ sakyesu ibbhavādaṃ nipātesi.
“But what wrong have the Śākyas done to you, Ambaṣṭha?”

“Once, sir, I went to Kapilavatthu on some business for Pokkharasāti, my teacher, the Brahmin.
And I went to the Sakyan’s meeting-hall ( sandhāgāra).
At that time a lot of Sakyans and Sakyan youths were sitting on high seats in their meeting-hall, poking each other with their fingers, laughing and playing about together, and it seemed to me that they were just making fun of me, and no one offered me a seat.
With regard to this, sir Gotama, it is not proper, it is unseemly that these Śākyas, menials though they are, being menials, do not honor Brahmins, do not respect Brahmins, do not esteem Brahmins, do not pay homage to Brahmins, do not show reverence to Brahmins.”

This was the second time Ambaṣṭha accused the Sakyans of being menials.
265.Aparaddhunti aparajjhiṃsu. Ekamidāhanti ettha idanti nipātamattaṃ. Ekaṃ ahanti attho. Sandhāgāranti rajjaanusāsanasālā. Sakyāti abhisittarājāno . Sakyakumārāti anabhisittā. Uccesūti yathānurūpesu pallaṅkapīṭhakavettāsanaphalakacittattharaṇādibhedesu. Sañjagghantāti uppaṇḍanavasena mahāhasitaṃ hasantā. Saṃkīḷantāti hasitamatta karaṇaaṅgulisaṅghaṭṭanapāṇippahāradānādīni karontā. Mamaññeva maññeti evamahaṃ maññāmi, mamaññeva anuhasanti, na aññanti. Kasmā pana te evamakaṃsūti? Te kira ambaṭṭhassa kulavaṃsaṃ jānanti. Ayañca tasmiṃ samaye yāva pādantā olambetvā nivatthasāṭakassa ekena hatthena dussakaṇṇaṃ gahetvā khandhaṭṭhikaṃ nāmetvā mānamadena matto viya āgacchati. Tato – ‘‘passatha bho amhākaṃ dāsassa kaṇhāyanagottassa ambaṭṭhassa āgamanakāraṇa’’nti vadantā evamakaṃsu. Sopi attano kulavaṃsaṃ jānāti. Tasmā ‘‘mamaññeva maññe’’ti takkayittha. Āsanenāti ‘‘idamāsanaṃ, ettha nisīdāhī’’ti evaṃ āsanena nimantanaṃ nāma hoti, tathā na koci akāsi.
265. Aparaddhunti means aparajjhiṃsu.

Ekamidāhanti: Here, idaṃ is merely a particle.

Ekaṃ ahanti: The meaning is one day.

Sandhāgāranti: It is the hall for royal decrees, rajjaanusāsanasālā.

Sakyāti: It means anointed kings, abhisittarājāno.

Sakyakumārāti: It means the unanointed, anabhisittā.

Uccesūti: Like suitable couches, chairs, wicker seats, seat-planks, painted carpets, etc. (yathānurūpesu pallaṅkapīṭhakavettāsanaphalakacittattharaṇādibhedesu).

Sañjagghantāti: Laughing a great laugh with exaggerated gestures, uppaṇḍanavasena mahāhasitaṃ hasantā.

Saṃkīḷantāti: Making sounds of laughter, clapping hands, snapping fingers, and giving blows with the palm (hasitamatta karaṇaaṅgulisaṅghaṭṭanapāṇippahāradānādīni karontā).

Mamaññeva maññeti: They think only of themselves, thus I think, they laugh only at themselves, not at others.

Kasmā pana te evamakaṃsūti? Why did they do so? Because they know the family lineage of Ambaṭṭha.

Ayañca tasmiṃ samaye: And at that time, he comes staggering as if drunk with pride and arrogance, letting the edge of his worn robe drag down to his feet, holding the corner of his garment with one hand, lowering his shoulders.

Tato: Then, saying, "Look at the reason for the coming of Ambaṭṭha, the slave of us, the Kaṇhāyana-gotta," they did so.

Sopi attano kulavaṃsaṃ jānāti: He also knows his own family lineage.

Tasmā ‘‘mamaññeva maññe’’ti takkayittha: Therefore, he thought, "They think only of me."

Āsanenāti: By (offering) a seat means "This is a seat, please be seated." Thus, inviting by (offering) a seat is what it is called, but no one did so. (‘‘idamāsanaṃ, ettha nisīdāhī’’ti evaṃ āsanena nimantanaṃ nāma hoti, tathā na koci akāsi).
266
266. ‘‘Laṭukikāpi kho, ambaṭṭha, sakuṇikā sake kulāvake kāmalāpinī hoti. Sakaṃ kho panetaṃ, ambaṭṭha, sakyānaṃ yadidaṃ kapilavatthuṃ, nārahatāyasmā ambaṭṭho imāya appamattāya abhisajjitu’’nti. ‘‘Cattārome, bho gotama, vaṇṇā – khattiyā brāhmaṇā vessā suddā. Imesañhi, bho gotama, catunnaṃ vaṇṇānaṃ tayo vaṇṇā – khattiyā ca vessā ca suddā ca – aññadatthu brāhmaṇasseva paricārakā sampajjanti. Tayidaṃ, bho gotama , nacchannaṃ, tayidaṃ nappatirūpaṃ, yadime sakyā ibbhā santā ibbhā samānā na brāhmaṇe sakkaronti, na brāhmaṇe garuṃ karonti, na brāhmaṇe mānenti, na brāhmaṇe pūjenti, na brāhmaṇe apacāyantī’’ti. Itiha ambaṭṭho māṇavo idaṃ tatiyaṃ sakyesu ibbhavādaṃ nipātesi.
266. “But Ambaṭṭha, even the quail, that little bird, can talk as she likes on her own nest. Kapilavatthu is the Śākyans’ home, Ambaṭṭha. The esteemed Ambaṭṭha, they do not deserve censure for such a trifle.” “Reverend Gotama, there are four castes ( varṇa ): the Kṣatriyas, the Brāhmaṇas, the Vaiśyas, and the Śūdras.” And of these four castes ( varṇa ) three—the Kṣatriyas, the Vaiśyas, and the Śūdras—are entirely subservient to the Brāhmaṇas. With regard to this, Reverend Gotama, it is not proper that they should not pay homage to the Brāhmaṇas. This was the third time Ambaṭṭha accused the Śākyans of being menials.
266.Laṭukikāti khettaleḍḍūnaṃ antarenivāsinī khuddakasakuṇikā. Kulāvaketi nivāsanaṭṭhāne. Kāmalāpinīti yadicchakabhāṇinī, yaṃ yaṃ icchati taṃ taṃ lapati, na taṃ koci haṃso vā koñco vā moro vā āgantvā ‘‘kiṃ tvaṃ lapasī’ti nisedheti. Abhisajjitunti kodhavasena laggituṃ. Evaṃ vutte māṇavo – ‘‘ayaṃ samaṇo gotamo attano ñātake laṭukikasadise katvā amhe haṃsakoñcamorasadise karoti, nimmāno dāni jāto’’ti maññamāno uttari cattāro vaṇṇe dasseti.
266. Laṭukikā means a small bird living among field clods.

Kulāvaka means a dwelling place.

Kāmalāpinī means speaking as one wishes, whatever she desires, that she utters; no goose (haṃsa) or heron (koñca) or peacock (mora) comes and forbids her, saying, "Why do you prate?".

Abhisajjitunti means clinging through the force of anger (krodhavaśena).

When this was said, the youth, thinking, "This samaṇa Gotama, having made his relatives similar to a quail (laṭukikā), makes us similar to geese (haṃsa), herons (koñca), and peacocks (mora); he has now become conceited (nimmāna)," showed four further qualities.
267
267. Atha kho bhagavato etadahosi – ‘‘atibāḷhaṃ kho ayaṃ ambaṭṭho māṇavo sakyesu ibbhavādena nimmādeti, yaṃnūnāhaṃ gottaṃ puccheyya’’nti. Atha kho bhagavā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘kathaṃ gottosi, ambaṭṭhā’’ti? ‘‘Kaṇhāyanohamasmi, bho gotamā’’ti. Porāṇaṃ kho pana te ambaṭṭha mātāpettikaṃ nāmagottaṃ anussarato ayyaputtā sakyā bhavanti; dāsiputto tvamasi sakyānaṃ. Sakyā kho pana, ambaṭṭha, rājānaṃ okkākaṃ pitāmahaṃ dahanti. ‘‘Bhūtapubbaṃ, ambaṭṭha, rājā okkāko yā sā mahesī piyā manāpā, tassā puttassa rajjaṃ pariṇāmetukāmo jeṭṭhakumāre raṭṭhasmā pabbājesi – okkāmukhaṃ karakaṇḍaṃ ukkāmukhaṃ karakaṇḍuṃ (sī. syā.) hatthinikaṃ sinisūraṃ sinipuraṃ (sī. syā.). Te raṭṭhasmā pabbājitā himavantapasse pokkharaṇiyā tīre mahāsākasaṇḍo, tattha vāsaṃ kappesuṃ. Te jātisambhedabhayā sakāhi bhaginīhi saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ kappesuṃ. ‘‘Atha kho, ambaṭṭha, rājā okkāko amacce pārisajje āmantesi – ‘kahaṃ nu kho, bho, etarahi kumārā sammantī’ti? ‘Atthi, deva, himavantapasse pokkharaṇiyā tīre mahāsākasaṇḍo , tatthetarahi kumārā sammanti. Te jātisambhedabhayā sakāhi bhaginīhi saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ kappentī’ti. Atha kho, ambaṭṭha, rājā okkāko udānaṃ udānesi – ‘sakyā vata, bho, kumārā, paramasakyā vata, bho, kumārā’ti. Tadagge kho pana ambaṭṭha sakyā paññāyanti; so ca nesaṃ pubbapuriso. ‘‘Rañño kho pana, ambaṭṭha, okkākassa disā nāma dāsī ahosi. Sā kaṇhaṃ nāma sā kaṇhaṃ (pī.) janesi. Jāto kaṇho pabyāhāsi – ‘dhovatha maṃ, amma, nahāpetha maṃ amma, imasmā maṃ asucismā parimocetha, atthāya vo bhavissāmī’ti. Yathā kho pana ambaṭṭha etarahi manussā pisāce disvā ‘pisācā’ti sañjānanti; evameva kho, ambaṭṭha, tena kho pana samayena manussā pisāce ‘kaṇhā’ti sañjānanti. Te evamāhaṃsu – ‘ayaṃ jāto pabyāhāsi, kaṇho jāto, pisāco jāto’ti. Tadagge kho pana, ambaṭṭha kaṇhāyanā paññāyanti, so ca kaṇhāyanānaṃ pubbapuriso. Iti kho te , ambaṭṭha, porāṇaṃ mātāpettikaṃ nāmagottaṃ anussarato ayyaputtā sakyā bhavanti, dāsiputto tvamasi sakyāna’’nti.
Then the Lord thought: ‘This Ambaṭṭha youth is greatly disparaging the Śākyas with talk of slaves. Suppose I were to ask his gotra (clan, lineage)?’

Then the Lord said this to the youth Ambaṭṭha: “What is your gotra (clan, lineage), Ambaṭṭha?”

“I am a Kāṇhāyana, O Gautama.”

“Ambaṭṭha, according to those who remember your ancient maternal and paternal lineage, the Śākyas are of noble birth; you are the son of a slave girl of the Śākyas.

For the Śākyas regard King Okkāka as their grandfather (pitāmaha).

“Once, Ambaṭṭha, King Okkāka, wishing to transfer the kingdom to the son of his beloved and pleasing queen, banished his eldest sons from the kingdom—Okkāmukha, Karakaṇḍa, Hatthinika, and Sinisūra.

These, banished from the kingdom, made their home on the flank of the Himalayas beside a lotus-pond where there was a big grove of teak-trees.

And for fear of contaminating the stock, they cohabited with their own sisters.

Then King Okkāka addressed his ministers and courtiers: ‘Where, pray, are the princes now assembled?’

‘There is, O King, on the flank of the Himalayas beside a lotus-pond, a big grove of teak-trees, and there the princes are now assembled.

For fear of contaminating the stock, they are cohabiting with their own sisters.’

Then King Okkāka exclaimed: ‘They are truly sakyas (able ones), these princes! They are supremely sakyas (able ones), these princes!’

From that time forth, Ambaṭṭha, they were known as the Śākyas; and he was their ancient ancestor.

Now King Okkāka had a slave-girl named Disā.

She gave birth to a black (child) (kaṇha).

As soon as he was born, the black thing exclaimed: ‘Wash me, mother! Bathe me, mother! Deliver me from this impurity, and I will bring you profit!’

Because, Ambaṭṭha, just as people today, seeing hobgoblins (pisācas), recognize them as hobgoblins, so in those days people recognized hobgoblins as ‘black ones’ (kaṇhas).

And they said: ‘As soon as he was born, he spoke. He is born a kaṇha (black one), a pisāca (hobgoblin)!’

From that time forth, Ambaṭṭha, they were known as the Kāṇhāyanas; and he was the ancient ancestor of the Kāṇhāyanas.

Thus, Ambaṭṭha, according to those who remember your ancient maternal and paternal lineage, the Śākyas are of noble birth; you are the son of a slave girl of the Śākyas.”
267.Nimmādetīti nimmadeti nimmāne karoti. Yaṃnūnāhanti yadi panāhaṃ. ‘‘Kaṇhāyanohamasmi, bho gotamā’’ti idaṃ kira vacanaṃ ambaṭṭho tikkhattuṃ mahāsaddena avoca. Kasmā avoca? Kiṃ asuddhabhāvaṃ na jānātīti? Āma jānāti. Jānantopi bhavapaṭicchannametaṃ kāraṇaṃ, taṃ anena na diṭṭhaṃ. Apassanto mahāsamaṇo kiṃ vakkhatīti maññamāno mānathaddhatāya avoca. Mātāpettikanti mātāpitūnaṃ santakaṃ. Nāmagottanti paṇṇattivasena nāmaṃ, paveṇīvasena gottaṃ. Anussaratoti anussarantassa kulakoṭiṃ sodhentassa. Ayyaputtāti sāmino puttā. Dāsiputtoti gharadāsiyāva putto. Tasmā yathā dāsena sāmino upasaṅkamitabbā, evaṃ anupasaṅkamantaṃ taṃ disvā sakyā anujagghiṃsūti dasseti. Ito paraṃ tassa dāsabhāvaṃ sakyānañca sāmibhāvaṃ pakāsetvā attano ca ambaṭṭhassa ca kulavaṃsaṃ āharanto sakyā kho panātiādimāha. Tattha dahantīti ṭhapenti, okkāko no pubbapurisoti, evaṃ karontīti attho. Tassa kira rañño kathanakāle ukkā viya mukhato pabhā niccharati, tasmā taṃ ‘‘okkāko’’ti sañjāniṃsūti. Pabbājesīti nīhari. Idāni te nāmavasena dassento – ‘‘okkāmukha’’ntiādimāha. Tatrāyaṃ anupubbī kathā – paṭhamakappikānaṃ kira rañño mahāsammatassa rojo nāma putto ahosi. Rojassa vararojo, vararojassa kalyāṇo, kalyāṇassa varakalyāṇo, varakalyāṇassa mandhātā, mandhātussa varamandhātā , varamandhātussa uposatho, uposathassa varo, varassa upavaro, upavarassa maghadevo, maghadevassa paramparāya caturāsītikhattiyasahassāni ahesuṃ. Tesaṃ pacchato tayo okkākavaṃsā ahesuṃ. Tesu tatiyaokkākassa pañca mahesiyo ahesuṃ – hatthā, cittā, jantu, jālinī, visākhāti. Ekekissā pañcapañcaitthisataparivārā. Sabbajeṭṭhāya cattāro puttā – okkāmukho, karakaṇḍu, hatthiniko, sinisūroti. Pañca dhītaro – piyā, suppiyā, ānandā, vijitā, vijitasenāti. Iti sā nava putte vijāyitvā kālamakāsi. Atha rājā aññaṃ dahariṃ abhirūpaṃ rājadhītaraṃ ānetvā aggamahesiṭṭhāne ṭhapesi. Sā jantuṃ nāma puttaṃ vijāyi. Atha naṃ pañcamadivase alaṅkaritvā rañño dassesi. Rājā tuṭṭho tassā varaṃ adāsi. Sā ñātakehi saddhiṃ mantetvā puttassa rajjaṃ yāci. Rājā – ‘‘nassa, vasali, mama puttānaṃ antarāyaṃ icchasī’’ti tajjesi. Sā punappunaṃ raho rājānaṃ paritosetvā – ‘‘mahārāja, musāvādo nāma na vaṭṭatī’’tiādīni vatvā yācatiyeva. Atha rājā putte āmantesi – ‘‘ahaṃ tātā, tumhākaṃ kaniṭṭhaṃ jantukumāraṃ disvā tassa mātuyā sahasā varaṃ adāsiṃ , sā puttassa rajjaṃ pariṇāmetuṃ icchati. Tumhe ṭhapetvā maṅgalahatthiṃ maṅgalaassaṃ maṅgalarathañca yattake icchatha, tattake hatthiassarathe gahetvā gacchatha. Mamaccayena āgantvā rajjaṃ kareyyāthā’’ti, aṭṭhahi amaccehi saddhiṃ uyyojesi. Te nānappakāraṃ roditvā kanditvā – ‘‘tāta, amhākaṃ dosaṃ khamathā’’ti rājānañceva rājorodhe ca khamāpetvā, ‘‘mayampi bhātūhi saddhiṃ gacchāmā’’ti rājānaṃ āpucchitvā nagarā nikkhantā bhaginiyo ādāya caturaṅginiyā senāya parivutā nagarā nikkhamiṃsu. ‘‘Kumārā pituaccayena āgantvā rajjaṃ kāressanti, gacchāma ne upaṭṭhahāmā’’ti cintetvā bahū manussā anubandhiṃsu. Paṭhamadivase yojanamattā senā ahosi, dutiye dviyojanamattā, tatiye tiyojanamattā. Kumārā mantayiṃsu – ‘‘mahā balakāyo, sace mayaṃ kañci sāmantarājānaṃ madditvā janapadaṃ gaṇheyyāma, sopi no nappasaheyya. Kiṃ paresaṃ pīḷāya katāya, mahā ayaṃ jambudīpo, araññe nagaraṃ māpessāmā’’ti himavantābhimukhā gantvā nagaravatthuṃ pariyesiṃsu. Tasmiñca samaye amhākaṃ bodhisatto brāhmaṇamahāsālakule nibbattitvā kapilabrāhmaṇo nāma hutvā nikkhamma isipabbajjaṃ pabbajitvā himavantapasse pokkharaṇiyā tīre sākavanasaṇḍe paṇṇasālaṃ māpetvā vasati. So kira bhummajālaṃ nāma vijjaṃ jānāti, yāya uddhaṃ asītihatthe ākāse, heṭṭhā ca bhūmiyampi guṇadosaṃ passati. Etasmiṃ padese tiṇagumbalatā dakkhiṇāvaṭṭā pācīnābhimukhā jāyanti. Sīhabyagghādayo migasūkare sappabiḷārā ca maṇḍūkamūsike anubandhamānā taṃ padesaṃ patvā na sakkonti te anubandhituṃ. Tehi te aññadatthu santajjitā nivattantiyeva. So – ‘‘ayaṃ pathaviyā aggapadeso’’ti ñatvā tattha attano paṇṇasālaṃ māpesi. Atha te kumāre nagaravatthuṃ pariyesamāne attano vasanokāsaṃ āgate disvā pucchitvā taṃ pavattiṃ ñatvā tesu anukampaṃ janetvā avoca – ‘‘imasmiṃ paṇṇasālaṭṭhāne māpitaṃ nagaraṃ jambudīpe agganagaraṃ bhavissati. Ettha jātapurisesu ekeko purisasatampi purisasahassampi abhibhavituṃ sakkhissati. Ettha nagaraṃ māpetha, paṇṇasālaṭṭhāne rañño gharaṃ karotha. Imasmiñhi okāse ṭhatvā caṇḍālaputtopi cakkavattibalena atiseyyo’’ti. Nanu, bhante, ayyassa vasanokāsoti? ‘‘Mama vasanokāso’’ti mā cintayittha. Mayhaṃ ekapasse paṇṇasālaṃ katvā nagaraṃ māpetvā kapilavatthunti nāmaṃ karothā’’ti. Te tathā katvā tattha nivāsaṃ kappesuṃ. Athāmaccā – ‘‘ime dārakā vayappattā, sace nesaṃ pitā santike bhaveyya, so āvāhavivāhaṃ kareyya. Idāni pana amhākaṃ bhāro’’ti cintetvā kumārehi saddhiṃ mantayiṃsu. Kumārā amhākaṃ sadisā khattiyadhītaro nāma na passāma, nāpi bhaginīnaṃ sadise khattiyakumārake, asadisasaṃyoge ca no uppannā puttā mātito vā pitito vā aparisuddhā jātisambhedaṃ pāpuṇissanti. Tasmā mayaṃ bhaginīhiyeva saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ rocemāti. Te jātisambhedabhayena jeṭṭhakabhaginiṃ mātuṭṭhāne ṭhapetvā avasesāhi saṃvāsaṃ kappesuṃ. Tesaṃ puttehi ca dhītāhi ca vaḍḍhamānānaṃ aparena samayena jeṭṭhakabhaginiyā kuṭṭharogo udapādi, koviḷārapupphasadisāni gattāni ahesuṃ. Rājakumārā imāya saddhiṃ ekato nisajjaṭṭhānabhojanādīni karontānampi upari ayaṃ rogo saṅkamatīti cintetvā ekadivasaṃ uyyānakīḷaṃ gacchantā viya taṃ yāne āropetvā araññaṃ pavisitvā bhūmiyaṃ pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇāpetvā tattha khādanīyabhojanīyena saddhiṃ taṃ pakkhipitvā gharasaṅkhepena upari padaraṃ paṭicchādetvā paṃsuṃ datvā pakkamiṃsu. Tena ca samayena rāmo nāma bārāṇasirājā kuṭṭharogo nāṭakitthīhi ca orodhehi ca jigucchiyamāno tena saṃvegena jeṭṭhaputtassa rajjaṃ datvā araññaṃ pavisitvā tattha paṇṇasālaṃ māpetvā mūlaphalāni paribhuñjanto nacirasseva arogo suvaṇṇavaṇṇo hutvā ito cito ca vicaranto mahantaṃ susirarukkhaṃ disvā tassabbhantare soḷasahatthappamāṇaṃ okāsaṃ sodhetvā dvārañca vātapānañca yojetvā nisseṇiṃ bandhitvā tattha vāsaṃ kappesi. So aṅgārakaṭāhe aggiṃ katvā rattiṃ migasūkarādīnaṃ sadde suṇanto sayati. So – ‘‘asukasmiṃ padese sīho saddamakāsi, asukasmiṃ byaggho’’ti sallakkhetvā pabhāte tattha gantvā vighāsamaṃsaṃ ādāya pacitvā khādati. Athekadivasaṃ tasmiṃ paccūsasamaye aggiṃ jāletvā nisinne rājadhītāya sarīragandhena āgantvā byaggho tasmiṃ padese paṃsuṃ viyūhanto padare vivaramakāsi, tena ca vivarena sā byagghaṃ disvā bhītā vissaramakāsi. So taṃ saddaṃ sutvā – ‘‘itthisaddo eso’’ti ca sallakkhetvā pātova tattha gantvā – ‘‘ko etthā’’ti āha. Mātugāmo sāmīti. Kiṃ jātikāsīti? Okkākamahārājassa dhītā sāmīti. Nikkhamāti? Na sakkā sāmīti. Kiṃ kāraṇāti? Chavirogo me atthīti. So sabbaṃ pavattiṃ pucchitvā khattiyamānena anikkhamantiṃ – ‘‘ahampi khattiyo’’ti attano khattiyabhāvaṃ jānāpetvā nisseṇiṃ datvā uddharitvā attano vasanokāsaṃ netvā sayaṃ paribhuttabhesajjāniyeva datvā nacirasseva arogaṃ suvaṇṇavaṇṇaṃ katvā tāya saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ kappesi. Sā paṭhamasaṃvāseneva gabbhaṃ gaṇhitvā dve putte vijāyi, punapi dveti, evaṃ soḷasakkhattumpi vijāyi. Evaṃ dvattiṃsa bhātaro ahesuṃ. Te anupubbena vuḍḍhippatte pitā sabbasippāni sikkhāpesi. Athekadivasaṃ eko rāmarañño nagaravāsī vanacarako pabbate ratanāni gavesanto rājānaṃ disvā sañjānitvā āha – ‘‘jānāmahaṃ, deva, tumhe’’ti. Tato naṃ rājā sabbaṃ pavattiṃ pucchi. Tasmiṃyeva ca khaṇe te dārakā āgamiṃsu. So te disvā – ‘‘ke ime’’ti āha. ‘‘Puttā me’’ti ca vutte tesaṃ mātikavaṃsaṃ pucchitvā – ‘‘laddhaṃ dāni me pābhata’’nti nagaraṃ gantvā rañño ārocesi. So ‘pitaraṃ ānayissāmī’ti caturaṅginiyā senāya tattha gantvā pitaraṃ vanditvā – ‘‘rajjaṃ, deva, sampaṭicchā’’ti yāci. So – ‘‘alaṃ, tāta, na tattha gacchāmi, idheva me imaṃ rukkhaṃ apanetvā nagaraṃ māpehī’’ti āha. So tathā katvā tassa nagarassa kolarukkhaṃ apanetvā katattā kolanagaranti ca byagghapathe katattā byagghapathanti cāti dve nāmāni āropetvā pitaraṃ vanditvā attano nagaraṃ agamāsi. Tato vayappatte kumāre mātā āha – ‘‘tātā, tumhākaṃ kapilavatthuvāsino sakyā mātulā santi. Mātuladhītānaṃ pana vo evarūpaṃ nāma kesaggahaṇaṃ hoti, evarūpaṃ dussagahaṇaṃ. Yadā tā nhānatitthaṃ āgacchanti, tadā gantvā yassa yā ruccati, so taṃ gaṇhatū’’ti. Te tatheva gantvā tāsu nhatvā sīsaṃ sukkhāpayamānāsu yaṃ yaṃ icchiṃsu, taṃ taṃ gahetvā nāmaṃ sāvetvā agamiṃsu. Sakyarājāno sutvā ‘‘hotu, bhaṇe, amhākaṃ ñātakā eva te’’ti tuṇhī ahesuṃ. Ayaṃ sakyakoliyānaṃ uppatti. Evaṃ tesaṃ sakyakoliyānaṃ aññamaññaṃ āvāhavivāhaṃ karontānaṃ yāva buddhakālā anupacchinnova vaṃso āgato. Tattha bhagavā sakyavaṃsaṃ dassetuṃ – ‘‘te raṭṭhasmā pabbājitā himavantapasse pokkharaṇiyā tīre’’tiādimāha. Tattha sammantīti vasanti. Sakyā vata bhoti raṭṭhasmā pabbājitā araññe vasantāpi jātisambhedamakatvā kulavaṃsaṃ anurakkhituṃ sakyā, samatthā, paṭibalāti attho. Tadaggeti taṃ aggaṃ katvā, tato paṭṭhāyāti attho. So ca nesaṃ pubbapurisoti so okkāko rājā etesaṃ pubbapuriso. Natthi etesaṃ gahapativaṃsena sambhedamattampīti. Evaṃ sakyavaṃsaṃ pakāsetvā idāni ambaṭṭhavaṃsaṃ pakāsento – ‘‘rañño kho panā’’tiādimāha. Kaṇhaṃnāma janesīti kāḷavaṇṇaṃ antokucchiyaṃyeva sañjātadantaṃ parūḷhamassudāṭhikaṃ puttaṃ vijāyi. Pabyāhāsīti yakkho jātoti bhayena palāyitvā dvāraṃ pidhāya ṭhitesu gharamānusakesu ito cito ca vicaranto dhovatha mantiādīni vadanto uccāsaddamakāsi.
267. "Nimmādeti" means "nimmadeti," he makes a creation.
"Yaṃnūnāhanti," if indeed I were to strike.
"Kaṇhāyana, I am, Gotama!" Thus, Ambaṭṭha spoke this utterance three times in a loud voice.
Why did he speak? Does he not know the impure state? Yes, he knows.
Although knowing, this cause is concealed by existence, it was not seen by him.
Thinking, "What will the great ascetic say?" he spoke in arrogance.
"Mātāpettika" means belonging to mother and father (mātāpitūnaṃ santakaṃ).
"Nāmagotta" means name according to designation, lineage according to tradition.
"Anussarato" means remembering, purifying the family lineage.
"Ayyaputta" means sons of the master.
"Dāsiputta" means son of a household slave.
Therefore, just as a slave should approach a master, showing that the Śākyas laughed when seeing him not approaching in that way.
Henceforth, revealing his slave status and the Śākyas' master status, and bringing forth his own and Ambaṭṭha's family lineage, he said, "Śākyas, indeed..." and so on.
There, "dahanti" means they place, "Okkāko is our ancestor," this is the meaning of doing thus.
It is said that at the time of that king's speaking, light emanated from his mouth like a torch, therefore they recognized him as "Okkāko."
"Pabbājesi" means he expelled (nīhari).
Now, showing according to the meaning of the name, he said, "Okkāmukha," and so forth.
There, this is the sequential story: It is said that for the first aeon, King Mahāsammata had a son named Roja.
Roja's son was Vararoja, Vararoja's son was Kalyāṇa, Kalyāṇa's son was Varakalyāṇa, Varakalyāṇa's son was Mandhātā, Mandhātā's son was Varamandhātā, Varamandhātā's son was Uposatha, Uposatha's son was Vara, Vara's son was Upavara, Upavara's son was Maghadeva. In Maghadeva's lineage, there were eighty-four thousand Kṣatriya kings.
After them, there were three Okkāka lineages.
Among them, Tatiya Okkāka had five queens: Hatthā, Cittā, Jantu, Jālinī, Visākhā.
Each one had five hundred female attendants.
The eldest had four sons: Okkāmukha, Karakaṇḍu, Hatthinika, Sinisūra.
Five daughters: Piyā, Suppiyā, Ānandā, Vijitā, Vijitasenā.
Thus, having given birth to nine sons, she died.
Then the king brought another young, beautiful princess and placed her in the position of chief queen.
She gave birth to a son named Jantu.
Then on the fifth day, having adorned him, she showed him to the king.
The king, pleased, gave her a boon.
Having consulted with her relatives, she asked for the kingdom for her son.
The king rebuked, "Wretched woman, do you desire harm for my sons?"
Again and again, having pleased the king in secret, she said, "Great king, a lie is not proper," and so on, she kept asking.
Then the king addressed the sons, "Dear ones, having seen your younger brother, Prince Jantu, I suddenly gave a boon to his mother, she desires to transfer the kingdom to her son.
Leaving you, taking as many auspicious elephants, auspicious horses, and auspicious chariots as you wish, go.
Having come after my death, may you rule the kingdom!", with eight ministers, he sent them away.
Weeping and lamenting in various ways, "Dear father, forgive our offense!", having asked forgiveness of the king and the royal women, "We too will go with our brothers," having taken leave of the king, the sisters, surrounded by a fourfold army, departed from the city.
"The princes will come and rule the kingdom after the father's death, let us go and attend to them," thinking thus, many people followed.
On the first day, there was an army of one yojana, on the second day, two yojanas, on the third day, three yojanas.
The princes consulted, "There is a great army, if we were to crush any neighboring king and seize a country, even he would not be able to withstand us."
Having inflicted suffering on others, [they thought,] "Great is this Jambudvīpa, we shall have a city built in the wilderness," and having gone towards the Himālaya, they sought a site for a city.

At that time, our Bodhisattva, having been born in a Brāhman great-wealthy family, having become a Brāhman named Kapila, having gone forth, having taken the ascetic life of a hermit, lived near the Himālaya mountains, on the bank of a lotus pond, in a grove of teak trees, having built a leaf hut.

It is said that he knows a science called Bhummajāla, by which, upwards eighty cubits in the sky, and downwards on the earth, he sees good and bad qualities.

In this region, grass, bushes, and creepers grow turning to the right, facing the east.

Lions, tigers, and the like, wild boars, snakes and cats, chasing frogs and mice, having reached that region, are not able to chase them.

By those [spirits of the place], they are frightened and turn back.

He, knowing that "this is the highest place on earth," built his leaf hut there.

Then those boys, seeking a site for a city, seeing [him] having come to their dwelling place, having asked [him] and learned that story, having generated compassion for them, said: "In this place of the leaf hut, the city built will be the chief city in Jambudvīpa.

Here, among the men born, each one will be able to overcome a hundred or a thousand men.

Build a city here, in the place of the leaf hut, make a house for the king.

For, having stood in this place, even a Caṇḍāla's son will be superior through the power of a universal monarch."

"Sir, is this a suitable dwelling place for the noble one?" "Do not think, 'This is my dwelling place.'"

Having made a leaf hut on one side for me, having built a city, give it the name Kapilavatthu."

Having done so, they arranged a dwelling there.

Then the ministers [thought,] "These boys have reached adulthood, if their father were nearby, he would perform the marriage ceremony.

Now, however, [they are] a burden to us," having thought, they consulted with the boys.

The boys said, "We do not see Kṣatriya daughters equal to us, nor Kṣatriya boys equal to [our] sisters, and in an unequal union, the sons born to us, from mother or father, will attain an impure mixture of castes.

Therefore, let us be pleased with living together with our own sisters."

Through fear of a mixture of castes, having placed the eldest sister in the position of mother, they arranged to live together with the remaining sisters.

As their sons and daughters grew up, after some time, the eldest sister contracted leprosy, her limbs became like koviḷāra flowers.

The princes, thinking that "this disease is spreading even to those sitting, standing, eating, and the like together with her," one day, as if going to a garden sporting event, having put her in a vehicle, having entered the forest, having had a lotus pond dug in the ground, having thrown her there together with food and provisions, having covered [the pond] above with a covering like a house, having given earth, they departed.

And at that time, a king of Vārāṇasī named Rāma, disgusted with leprosy by dancing women and the women of the harem, through that agitation, having given the kingdom to his eldest son, having entered the forest, having built a leaf hut there, enjoying roots and fruits, before long, having become healthy, golden-colored, wandering here and there, seeing a large hollow tree, having cleared a space of sixteen cubits inside it, and having joined a door and a window, having fastened a ladder, he arranged a dwelling there.

He, having made a fire in a charcoal brazier, sleeps at night, hearing the sounds of wild boars and the like.

He, having discerned – "In such and such a place a lion made a sound, in such and such a place a tiger," having gone there at dawn, having taken the remains of food, meat, having cooked [it], eats [it].

Then one day, at that time of early morning, while he was sitting having lit a fire, a tiger, drawn by the scent of the body of the princess, scattering the dust in that place, made a hole in the covering, and through that hole, she, seeing the tiger, terrified, made a loud noise.

He, having heard that sound, and having discerned – "This is a woman's voice," having gone there early in the morning, said, "Who is here?"

"A woman, sir."

"What is your caste?" "A daughter of King Okkāka, sir."

"Can you go out?" "I cannot, sir."

"What is the reason?" "I have a skin disease."

He, having asked about the whole story, not letting [her] go out due to Kṣatriya pride – having made known his Kṣatriya status, having given a ladder, having lifted [her] up, having led [her] to his dwelling place, having given the very medicines that he himself had taken, before long, having made [her] healthy, golden-colored, he lived together with her.

She, at the very first cohabitation, having conceived, bore two sons, again two, thus even sixteen times she bore [children].

Thus there were thirty-two brothers.

As they gradually reached adulthood, the father taught [them] all the skills.

Then one day, a forest wanderer, a resident of the city of the Rāma king, seeking jewels in the mountains, seeing the king, having recognized [him], said – "I know you, Your Majesty."

Then the king asked him about the whole story.

At that very moment, those boys came.

He, seeing them, said – "Who are these?"

"My sons," when [he] had said, having asked about their maternal lineage – "Now I have obtained a gift," having gone to the city, he announced [it] to the king.

He, thinking, "I will bring [my] father," having gone there with a four-part army, having greeted his father, begged – "Accept the kingdom, Your Majesty."
So [he said], "Enough, dear father, I will not go there. Have this tree removed and build a city for me here."

Having done so, he removed the kola tree from that city, and because he had it done, it was called Kolanagara, and because it was done on the Byagghapatha, it was called Byagghapatha. Thus, having bestowed two names, he paid homage to his father and went to his own city.

Then, when the boy reached adulthood, his mother said, "Dear son, you have maternal uncles, the Śākyas, who live in Kapilavastu.

For the daughters of your maternal uncles, there is such a custom as hair-taking, and such a custom as cloth-taking.

When they come to the bathing place, then go there, and whichever one pleases you, take her."

They went there, and having bathed in those [waters], while they were drying their heads, whatever they desired, they took that, announced [their] name, and left.

The Śākya kings, having heard, [said,] "Let it be, good sir, they are indeed our relatives," and they remained silent.

This is the origin of the Śākya-Koliyas.

Thus, as these Śākya-Koliyas were performing marriage ceremonies with each other, down to the time of the Buddha, the lineage came uninterrupted.

There, the Blessed One, to show the Śākya lineage, said, "They were banished from the kingdom, on the side of the Himavant, on the shore of a lotus pond," and so forth.

There, they are in harmony, so they dwell.

The Śākyas, though banished from the kingdom, dwelling in the forest, without making a mixture of caste, are capable of protecting the family lineage; they are able, they are fit. Such is the meaning.

'From that summit' means having made that the summit. From then on, such is the meaning.

And that one was their ancestor; that Okkāka king was their ancestor.

There is not even a little bit of mixing with a householder lineage for them.

Thus, having revealed the Śākya lineage, now, revealing the Āmbaṣṭha lineage, he said, "Indeed, for the king," and so on.

'She gave birth to one named Kanhha' means she gave birth to a black-colored son, with teeth already formed inside the womb, with overgrown beard and fangs.

'He shouted' means that a yakṣa was born. Because of fear, having fled and when the householders were standing having closed the door, wandering here and there, shouting, "Wash it! Incantations!" and so forth, he made a loud noise.
268
268. Evaṃ vutte, te māṇavakā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘‘mā bhavaṃ gotamo ambaṭṭhaṃ atibāḷhaṃ dāsiputtavādena nimmādesi. Sujāto ca, bho gotama ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kulaputto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, bahussuto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kalyāṇavākkaraṇo ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, paṇḍito ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, pahoti ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhotā gotamena saddhiṃ asmiṃ vacane paṭimantetu’’nti.
On hearing this, the young men said to the Blessed One: "Venerable Gotama, do not humiliate Ambaṭṭha too much by calling him a son of a slave girl ( dāsiputtavādena nimmādesi).
Ambaṭṭha, Reverend Gotama, is well-born ( sujāto ca), Ambaṭṭha is of good family ( kulaputto ca), Ambaṭṭha is very learned ( bahussuto ca), Ambaṭṭha is well-spoken ( kalyāṇavākkaraṇo ca), Ambaṭṭha is a scholar ( paṇḍito ca), Ambaṭṭha is capable of holding his own in this discussion with the Reverend Gotama!"
268.Temāṇavakā bhagavantaṃ etadavocunti attano upārambhamocanatthāya – ‘‘etaṃ mā bhava’’ntiādivacanaṃ avocuṃ. Tesaṃ kira etadahosi – ‘‘ambaṭṭho amhākaṃ ācariyassa jeṭṭhantevāsī, sace mayaṃ evarūpe ṭhāne ekadvevacanamattampi na vakkhāma, ayaṃ no ācariyassa santike amhe paribhindissatī’’ti upārambhamocanatthaṃ evaṃ avocuṃ. Cittena panassa nimmadabhāvaṃ ākaṅkhanti. Ayaṃ kira mānanissitattā tesampi appiyova. Kalyāṇavākkaraṇoti madhuravacano. Asmiṃ vacaneti attanā uggahite vedattayavacane. Paṭimantetunti pucchitaṃ pañhaṃ paṭikathetuṃ, vissajjetunti attho. Etasmiṃ vā dāsiputtavacane. Paṭimantetunti uttaraṃ kathetuṃ.
268. The youths spoke thus to the Blessed One to avoid reproach for themselves: “Let this not be,” and they spoke similar words.

It occurred to them, “Ambaṭṭha is the senior pupil of our teacher. If we do not speak even a single word or two in such a situation, he will disparage us in the presence of our teacher.” Thus, they spoke in this way to avoid reproach.

With their minds, they desired his freedom from arrogance.

Indeed, this one, due to being dependent on conceit, is also disliked by them.

Kalyāṇavākkaraṇo means one whose speech is sweet.

In this statement, in the vedattayavacana grasped by himself.

Paṭimantetunti means to answer a question that has been asked, to reply, to dismiss.

Or in this statement, “son of a slave woman.”

Paṭimantetunti means to speak a reply.
269
269. Atha kho bhagavā te māṇavake etadavoca – ‘‘sace kho tumhākaṃ māṇavakānaṃ evaṃ hoti – ‘dujjāto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, akulaputto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, appassuto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, akalyāṇavākkaraṇo ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, duppañño ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, na ca pahoti ambaṭṭho māṇavo samaṇena gotamena saddhiṃ asmiṃ vacane paṭimantetu’nti, tiṭṭhatu ambaṭṭho māṇavo, tumhe mayā saddhiṃ mantavho asmiṃ vacane. Sace pana tumhākaṃ māṇavakānaṃ evaṃ hoti – ‘sujāto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kulaputto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, bahussuto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kalyāṇavākkaraṇo ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, paṇḍito ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, pahoti ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo samaṇena gotamena saddhiṃ asmiṃ vacane paṭimantetu’nti, tiṭṭhatha tumhe; ambaṭṭho māṇavo mayā saddhiṃ paṭimantetū’’ti. ‘‘Sujāto ca, bho gotama, ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kulaputto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, bahussuto ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, kalyāṇavākkaraṇo ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, paṇḍito ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo, pahoti ca ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhotā gotamena saddhiṃ asmiṃ vacane paṭimantetuṃ, tuṇhī mayaṃ bhavissāma, ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhotā gotamena saddhiṃ asmiṃ vacane paṭimantetū’’ti.
269. Then the Blessed One said to those young men: "If you think that Ambaṭṭha is ill-born (dujjāto), not of good family (akulaputto), unlearned (appassuto), with unskillful speech (akalyāṇavākkaraṇo), unwise (duppañño), and unable to hold his own in this discussion with the śramaṇa (ascetic) Gotama, then let Ambaṭṭha be silent, and you confer with me in this matter.

But if you think that Ambaṭṭha is well-born (sujāto), of good family (kulaputto), very learned (bahussuto), with skillful speech (kalyāṇavākkaraṇo), wise (paṇḍito), and able to hold his own in this discussion with the śramaṇa Gotama, then you be silent, and let Ambaṭṭha confer with me."

"Ambaṭṭha is well-born (sujāto), Reverend Gotama, of good family (kulaputto), very learned (bahussuto), with skillful speech (kalyāṇavākkaraṇo), wise (paṇḍito), and able to hold his own in this discussion with the Reverend Gotama; we will be silent, let Ambaṭṭha confer with the Reverend Gotama in this matter."
269.Atha kho bhagavāti atha kho bhagavā – ‘‘sace ime māṇavakā ettha nisinnā evaṃ uccāsaddaṃ karissanti, ayaṃ kathā pariyosānaṃ na gamissati. Handa, ne nissadde katvā ambaṭṭheneva saddhiṃ kathemī’’ti te māṇavake etadavoca. Tattha mantavhoti mantayatha. Mayā saddhiṃ paṭimantetūti mayā saha kathetu. Evaṃ vutte māṇavakā cintayiṃsu – ‘‘ambaṭṭho tāva dāsiputtosīti vutte puna sīsaṃ ukkhipituṃ nāsakkhi. Ayaṃ kho jāti nāma dujjānā, sace aññampi kiñci samaṇo gotamo ‘tvaṃ dāso’ti vakkhati, ko tena saddhiṃ aḍḍaṃ karissati. Ambaṭṭho attanā baddhaṃ puṭakaṃ attanāva mocetū’’ti attānaṃ parimocetvā tasseva upari khipantā – ‘‘sujāto ca bho gotamā’’tiādimāhaṃsu.
269.
Then, indeed, the Blessed One said: "If these youths, sitting here, make such a loud noise, this conversation will not reach its conclusion."

"Come now, having made them silent, I will converse with Ambaṭṭha." Thus, he spoke to those youths.

"There, you should consult."

"Let him consult with me," means, "Let him speak with me."

Having been spoken to in this way, the youths considered: "When it was said, 'Ambaṭṭha, you are the son of a slave girl,' he was not able to lift up his head again."

"This birth is indeed difficult to know. If even another ascetic, Gotama, should say, 'You are a slave,' who would engage in strife with him?"

"Ambaṭṭha should himself untie the bundle bound by himself." Having released himself, and throwing it upon that very thing, they said, "Gotama is well-born," and so on.
270
270. Atha kho bhagavā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘ayaṃ kho pana te, ambaṭṭha, sahadhammiko pañho āgacchati, akāmā byākātabbo. Sace tvaṃ na byākarissasi, aññena vā aññaṃ paṭicarissasi, tuṇhī vā bhavissasi, pakkamissasi vā ettheva te sattadhā muddhā phalissati. Taṃ kiṃ maññasi, ambaṭṭha, kinti te sutaṃ brāhmaṇānaṃ vuddhānaṃ mahallakānaṃ ācariyapācariyānaṃ bhāsamānānaṃ kutopabhutikā kaṇhāyanā, ko ca kaṇhāyanānaṃ pubbapuriso’’ti? Evaṃ vutte, ambaṭṭho māṇavo tuṇhī ahosi. Dutiyampi kho bhagavā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘taṃ kiṃ maññasi, ambaṭṭha, kinti te sutaṃ brāhmaṇānaṃ vuddhānaṃ mahallakānaṃ ācariyapācariyānaṃ bhāsamānānaṃ kutopabhutikā kaṇhāyanā, ko ca kaṇhāyanānaṃ pubbapuriso’’ti? Dutiyampi kho ambaṭṭho māṇavo tuṇhī ahosi. Atha kho bhagavā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘byākarohi dāni ambaṭṭha, na dāni, te tuṇhībhāvassa kālo. Yo kho, ambaṭṭha, tathāgatena yāvatatiyakaṃ sahadhammikaṃ pañhaṃ puṭṭho na byākaroti, etthevassa sattadhā muddhā phalissatī’’ti.
Then the Blessed One said to Ambaṭṭha, the young Brahmin: "Now, Ambaṭṭha, a fundamental question comes to you, which you must answer even if you do not want to. If you do not answer, or evade the issue, or keep silent, or go away, your head will split into seven pieces right here. What do you think, Ambaṭṭha? Have you heard what the elderly and aged Brahmin teachers say about the origins of the Kaṇhāyanas, and who was the ancestor of the Kaṇhāyanas?" When this was said, Ambaṭṭha, the young Brahmin, was silent.

A second time, the Blessed One said to Ambaṭṭha, the young Brahmin: "What do you think, Ambaṭṭha? Have you heard what the elderly and aged Brahmin teachers say about the origins of the Kaṇhāyanas, and who was the ancestor of the Kaṇhāyanas?" A second time, Ambaṭṭha, the young Brahmin, was silent.

Then the Blessed One said to Ambaṭṭha, the young Brahmin: "Answer now, Ambaṭṭha, now is not the time for your silence. Whoever, Ambaṭṭha, does not answer a fundamental question put to him by a Tathāgata (Thus Gone One) by the third asking, his head will split into seven pieces right here."
270.Sahadhammikoti sahetuko sakāraṇo. Akāmā byākātabboti attanā anicchantenapi byākaritabbo, avassaṃ vissajjetabboti attho. Aññenavā aññaṃ paṭicarissasīti aññena vacanena aññaṃ vacanaṃ paṭicarissasi ajjhottharissasi, paṭicchādessasīti attho. Yo hi ‘‘kiṃ gotto tva’’nti evaṃ puṭṭho – ‘‘ahaṃ tayo vede jānāmī’’tiādīni vadati, ayaṃ aññena aññaṃ paṭicarati nāma. Pakkamissasi vāti pucchitaṃ pañhaṃ jānantova akathetukāmatāya uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkamissasi vā. Tuṇhīahosīti samaṇo gotamo maṃ sāmaṃyeva dāsiputtabhāvaṃ kathāpetukāmo, sāmaṃ kathite ca dāso nāma jātoyeva hoti. Ayaṃ pana dvatikkhattuṃ codetvā tuṇhī bhavissati, tato ahaṃ parivattitvā pakkamissāmīti cintetvā tuṇhī ahosi.
270. Sahadhammika means with a cause (sahetuka) and a reason (sakāraṇa).

Akāmā byākātabbo means even if one is unwilling, it should be explained; it definitely must be elucidated, that is the meaning.

Aññena vā aññaṃ paṭicarissasi means with one statement, you will obscure another statement; you will conceal [it], that is the meaning.

If someone, when asked, "What is your clan (gotta)?" says, "I know the three Vedas (veda)," etc., this is called obscuring one thing with another.

Pakkamissasi vā means, knowing the question (pañha) that was asked, one gets up from one's seat (āsana) and leaves, due to unwillingness to speak, or not.

Tuṇhī ahosī means the ascetic (samaṇa) Gotama wants me to state the condition of being the son of a slave woman (dāsiputtabhāva) myself, and if it is stated myself, one is born a slave (dāsa) indeed.

Furthermore, this one, having urged [me] twice or thrice, will become silent; then, thinking "Having turned around, I will leave," he became silent.
271
271. Tena kho pana samayena vajirapāṇī yakkho mahantaṃ ayokūṭaṃ ādāya ādittaṃ sampajjalitaṃ sajotibhūtaṃ sañjotibhūtaṃ (syā.) ambaṭṭhassa māṇavassa upari vehāsaṃ ṭhito hoti – ‘‘sacāyaṃ ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhagavatā yāvatatiyakaṃ sahadhammikaṃ pañhaṃ puṭṭho na byākarissati, etthevassa sattadhā muddhaṃ phālessāmī’’ti. Taṃ kho pana vajirapāṇiṃ yakkhaṃ bhagavā ceva passati ambaṭṭho ca māṇavo.
Verse 1.271:

At that time, Vajrapāṇi, the yakṣa, holding a massive iron club (ayokūṭa), blazing, intensely burning, and brilliantly glowing, was standing in the sky above Ambaṭṭha, the young brahmin, thinking, "If this young man Ambaṭṭha, questioned by the Blessed One with a proper question for the third time, does not answer, right here I will split his head into seven pieces!"

Both the Blessed One and the young brahmin Ambaṭṭha saw Vajrapāṇi, the yakṣa.
271. Vajiraṃ pāṇimhi assāti vajirapāṇi. Yakkhoti na yo vā so vā yakkho, sakko devarājāti veditabbo. Ādittanti aggivaṇṇaṃ. Sampajjalitanti suṭṭhu pajjalitaṃ. Sajotibhūtanti samantato jotibhūtaṃ, ekaggijālabhūtanti attho. Ṭhito hotīti mahantaṃ sīsaṃ, kandalamakuḷasadisā dāṭhā bhayānakāni akkhināsādīni evaṃ virūparūpaṃ māpetvā ṭhito. Kasmā panesa āgatoti? Diṭṭhivissajjāpanatthaṃ. Api ca – ‘‘ahañceva kho pana dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, pare ca me na ājāneyyu’’nti evaṃ dhammadesanāya appossukkabhāvaṃ āpanne bhagavati sakko mahābrahmunā saddhiṃ āgantvā – ‘‘bhagavā dhammaṃ desetha, tumhākaṃ āṇāya avattamāne mayaṃ vattāpessāma, tumhākaṃ dhammacakkaṃ hotu, amhākaṃ āṇācakka’’nti paṭiññaṃ akāsi. Tasmā – ‘‘ajja ambaṭṭhaṃ tāsetvā pañhaṃ vissajjāpessāmī’’ti āgato. Bhagavā ceva passati ambaṭṭho cāti yadi hi taṃ aññepi passeyyuṃ, taṃ kāraṇaṃ agaru assa, ‘‘ayaṃ samaṇo gotamo ambaṭṭhaṃ attano vāde anotarantaṃ ñatvā yakkhaṃ āvāhetvā dassesi, tato ambaṭṭho bhayena kathesī’’ti vadeyyuṃ. Tasmā bhagavā ceva passati ambaṭṭho ca. Tassa taṃ disvāva sakalasarīrato sedā mucciṃsu. Antokucchi viparivattamānā mahāravaṃ viravi . So ‘‘aññepi nu kho passantī’’ti olokento kassaci lomahaṃsamattampi nāddasa. Tato – ‘‘idaṃ bhayaṃ mameva uppannaṃ, sacāhaṃ yakkhoti vakkhāmi, ‘kiṃ tavameva akkhīni atthi, tvameva yakkhaṃ passasi, paṭhamaṃ yakkhaṃ adisvā samaṇena gotamena vādasaṅghaṭṭe pakkhittova yakkhaṃ passasī’ti vadeyyu’’nti cintetvā ‘‘na dāni me idha aññaṃ paṭisaraṇaṃ atthi, aññatra samaṇā gotamā’’ti maññamāno atha kho ambaṭṭho māṇavo…pe… bhagavantaṃ etadavoca.
271.

Vajiraṃ pāṇimhi assāti vajirapāṇi: Because he has the vajira in his hand, he is vajirapāṇi.

Yakkhoti na yo vā so vā yakkho, sakko devarājāti veditabbo: A yakkha is not just anyone; it should be understood as Śakra, the king of the gods (devarāja).

Ādittanti aggivaṇṇaṃ: Āditta means the color of fire (agni).

Sampajjalitanti suṭṭhu pajjalitaṃ: Sampajjalita means very well ignited.

Sajotibhūtanti samantato jotibhūtaṃ, ekaggijālabhūtanti attho: Sajotibhūta means shining in all directions, it means being like a single mass of flames.

Ṭhito hotīti mahantaṃ sīsaṃ, kandalamakuḷasadisā dāṭhā bhayānakāni akkhināsādīni evaṃ virūparūpaṃ māpetvā ṭhito: "He stood" means a great head, teeth like kandala buds, frightening eyes, nose, etc., thus he stood having created a deformed appearance.

Kasmā panesa āgatoti? Diṭṭhivissajjāpanatthaṃ: Why did he come here? To refute [Ambaṭṭha's] views.

Api ca – ‘‘ahañceva kho pana dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, pare ca me na ājāneyyu’’nti evaṃ dhammadesanāya appossukkabhāvaṃ āpanne bhagavati sakko mahābrahmunā saddhiṃ āgantvā – ‘‘bhagavā dhammaṃ desetha, tumhākaṃ āṇāya avattamāne mayaṃ vattāpessāma, tumhākaṃ dhammacakkaṃ hotu, amhākaṃ āṇācakka’’nti paṭiññaṃ akāsi: Moreover, when the Blessed One was disinclined to teach the Dharma, thinking, "Even if I were to teach the Dharma, others would not understand me," Śakra, along with Mahābrahmā, came and made a pledge: "Blessed One, teach the Dharma! If it does not proceed according to your command, we will make it proceed. Let your Dharma-wheel (dharmacakra) exist, and our command-wheel (āṇācakra) [as well]."

Tasmā – ‘‘ajja ambaṭṭhaṃ tāsetvā pañhaṃ vissajjāpessāmī’’ti āgato: Therefore, he came, thinking, "Today, having frightened Ambaṭṭha, I will make him answer the question."

Bhagavā ceva passati ambaṭṭho cāti yadi hi taṃ aññepi passeyyuṃ, taṃ kāraṇaṃ agaru assa, ‘‘ayaṃ samaṇo gotamo ambaṭṭhaṃ attano vāde anotarantaṃ ñatvā yakkhaṃ āvāhetvā dassesi, tato ambaṭṭho bhayena kathesī’’ti vadeyyuṃ: Both the Blessed One and Ambaṭṭha saw it. If others had seen it, that reason would be dishonorable. They would say, "This śramaṇa Gautama, knowing that Ambaṭṭha could not reach the end of his argument, invoked a yaksha and showed it, and then Ambaṭṭha spoke out of fear."

Tasmā bhagavā ceva passati ambaṭṭho ca: Therefore, both the Blessed One and Ambaṭṭha saw it.

Tassa taṃ disvāva sakalasarīrato sedā mucciṃsu: Having seen that, sweat dripped from his entire body.

Antokucchi viparivattamānā mahāravaṃ viravi: His entrails churned, and he cried out with a great roar.

So ‘‘aññepi nu kho passantī’’ti olokento kassaci lomahaṃsamattampi nāddasa: He looked around, thinking, "Do others also see [this]?", but he did not see even a hair standing on end in anyone.

Tato – ‘‘idaṃ bhayaṃ mameva uppannaṃ, sacāhaṃ yakkhoti vakkhāmi, ‘kiṃ tavameva akkhīni atthi, tvameva yakkhaṃ passasi, paṭhamaṃ yakkhaṃ adisvā samaṇena gotamena vādasaṅghaṭṭe pakkhittova yakkhaṃ passasī’ti vadeyyu’’nti cintetvā ‘‘na dāni me idha aññaṃ paṭisaraṇaṃ atthi, aññatra samaṇā gotamā’’ti maññamāno atha kho ambaṭṭho māṇavo…pe… bhagavantaṃ etadavoca: Then, thinking, "This fear has arisen in me alone. If I say 'It is a yaksha,' they will say, 'Do only you have eyes? Do only you see the yaksha? Having not seen the yaksha before, you only see the yaksha because the śramaṇa Gautama has placed you in a verbal conflict'," and thinking, "Now, I have no other refuge here, other than the śramaṇa Gautama," then Ambaṭṭha the youth... said this to the Blessed One.
272
272. Atha kho ambaṭṭho māṇavo bhīto saṃviggo lomahaṭṭhajāto bhagavantaṃyeva tāṇaṃ gavesī bhagavantaṃyeva leṇaṃ gavesī bhagavantaṃyeva saraṇaṃ gavesī – upanisīditvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘kimetaṃ kiṃ me taṃ (ka.) bhavaṃ gotamo āha? Punabhavaṃ gotamo bravitū’’ti brūtu (syā.). ‘‘Taṃ kiṃ maññasi, ambaṭṭha, kinti te sutaṃ brāhmaṇānaṃ vuddhānaṃ mahallakānaṃ ācariyapācariyānaṃ bhāsamānānaṃ kutopabhutikā kaṇhāyanā, ko ca kaṇhāyanānaṃ pubbapuriso’’ti? ‘‘Evameva me, bho gotama, sutaṃ yatheva bhavaṃ gotamo āha. Tatopabhutikā kaṇhāyanā; so ca kaṇhāyanānaṃ pubbapuriso’’ti.
272. Then, the young Brahmin Ambaṭṭha, frightened, agitated, and with his hair standing on end, sought refuge in the Blessed One, sought shelter in the Blessed One, sought protection in the Blessed One. Approaching and sitting near the Blessed One, he said to him: "What is it, what is that, Reverend Gotama, that you said? Let the Reverend Gotama speak again."

"What do you think, Ambaṭṭha? Have you heard what the old and aged Brahmin teachers, the teachers of teachers, say about where the Kaṇhāyanas originated, and who was the ancestor of the Kaṇhāyanas?"

"Yes, indeed, Reverend Gotama, I have heard it just as the Reverend Gotama says. That is where the Kaṇhāyanas came from; he was their ancestor."
272.Tāṇaṃ gavesīti tāṇaṃ gavesamāno. Leṇaṃ gavesīti leṇaṃ gavesamāno. Saraṇaṃ gavesīti saraṇaṃ gavesamāno. Ettha ca tāyati rakkhatīti tāṇaṃ. Nilīyanti etthāti leṇaṃ. Saratīti saraṇaṃ, bhayaṃ hiṃsati, viddhaṃsetīti attho. Upanisīditvāti upagamma heṭṭhāsane nisīditvā. Bravitūti vadatu.
Seeking refuge is seeking protection; seeking shelter is seeking a hiding place; seeking help is seeking aid. Here, tāṇa means because it protects and guards. Leṇa means because [one] hides in this place. Saraṇa means because it remembers, it injures fear, it destroys, this is the meaning. Upanisīditvā means having approached, having sat on a lower seat. Bravitu means let [him] speak.
273
273. Evaṃ vutte, te māṇavakā unnādino uccāsaddamahāsaddā ahesuṃ – ‘‘dujjāto kira, bho, ambaṭṭho māṇavo; akulaputto kira, bho, ambaṭṭho māṇavo; dāsiputto kira, bho, ambaṭṭho māṇavo sakyānaṃ. Ayyaputtā kira, bho, ambaṭṭhassa māṇavassa sakyā bhavanti. Dhammavādiṃyeva kira mayaṃ samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ apasādetabbaṃ amaññimhā’’ti.
273. When this was said, those youths, being noisy, became loudly clamorous: "Indeed, Ambaṭṭha the youth is ill-born, sir; Ambaṭṭha the youth is of ignoble family, sir; Ambaṭṭha the youth is the son of a slave girl of the Sakyans.
The Sakyans are masters of Ambaṭṭha the youth, sir.
Indeed, we thought the ascetic Gotama, who speaks the truth ( dharma), was to be disparaged."
273-274.Dakkhiṇajanapadanti dakkhiṇāpathoti pākaṭaṃ. Gaṅgāya dakkhiṇato pākaṭajanapadaṃ. Tadā kira dakkhiṇāpathe bahū brāhmaṇatāpasā honti, so tattha gantvā ekaṃ tāpasaṃ vattapaṭipattiyā ārādhesi. So tassa upakāraṃ disvā āha – ‘‘ambho, purisa, mantaṃ te demi, yaṃ icchasi, taṃ mantaṃ gaṇhāhī’’ti. So āha – ‘‘na me ācariya, aññena mantena, kiccaṃ atthi, yassānubhāvena āvudhaṃ na parivattati, taṃ me mantaṃ dehī’’ti. So – ‘‘bhadraṃ, bho’’ti tassa dhanuagamanīyaṃ ambaṭṭhaṃ nāma vijjaṃ adāsi, so taṃ vijjaṃ gahetvā tattheva vīmaṃsitvā – ‘‘idāni me manorathaṃ pūressāmī’’ti isivesaṃ gahetvā okkākassa santikaṃ gato. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘dakkhiṇajanapadaṃ gantvā brahmamante adhīyitvā rājānaṃ okkākaṃ upasaṅkamitvā’’ti. Ettha brahmamanteti ānubhāvasampannatāya seṭṭhamante. Ko nevaṃ’re ayaṃ mayhaṃ dāsiputtoti ko nu evaṃ are ayaṃ mama dāsiputto. Sotaṃ khurappanti so rājā taṃ māretukāmatāya sannahitaṃ saraṃ tassa mantānubhāvena neva khipituṃ na apanetuṃ sakkhi, tāvadeva sakalasarīre sañjātasedo bhayena vedhamāno aṭṭhāsi. Amaccāti mahāmaccā. Pārisajjāti itare parisāvacarā. Etadavocunti – ‘‘daṇḍakīrañño kisavacchatāpase aparaddhassa āvudhavuṭṭhiyā sakalaraṭṭhaṃ vinaṭṭhaṃ . Nāḷikero pañcasu tāpasasatesu ajjuno ca aṅgīrase aparaddho pathaviṃ bhinditvā nirayaṃ paviṭṭho’’ti cintayantā bhayena etaṃ sotthi, bhaddantetiādivacanaṃ avocuṃ. Sotthi bhavissati raññoti idaṃ vacanaṃ kaṇho ciraṃ tuṇhī hutvā tato anekappakāraṃ yācīyamāno – ‘‘tumhākaṃ raññā mādisassa isino khurappaṃ sannayhantena bhāriyaṃ kammaṃ kata’’ntiādīni ca vatvā pacchā abhāsi. Undriyissatīti bhijjissati, thusamuṭṭhi viya vippakiriyissatīti. Idaṃ so ‘‘janaṃ tāsessāmī’’ti musā bhaṇati. Sarasanthambhanamatteyeva hissa vijjāya ānubhāvo, na aññatra. Ito paresupi vacanesu eseva nayo. Pallomoti pannalomo. Lomahaṃsanamattampissa na bhavissati. Idaṃ kira so ‘‘sace me rājā taṃ dārikaṃ dassatī’’ti paṭiññaṃ kāretvā avaca. Kumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhapesīti tena ‘‘saro otaratū’’ti mante parivatti, te kumārassa nābhiyaṃ patiṭṭhapesi. Dhītaraṃ adāsīti sīsaṃ dhovitvā adāsaṃ bhujissaṃ katvā dhītaraṃ adāsi, uḷāre ca taṃ ṭhāne ṭhapesi. Mā kho tumhe māṇavakāti idaṃ pana bhagavā – ‘‘ekena pakkhena ambaṭṭho sakyānaṃ ñāti hotī’’ti pakāsento tassa samassāsanatthaṃ āha. Tato ambaṭṭho ghaṭasatena abhisitto viya passaddhadaratho hutvā samassāsetvā samaṇo gotamo maṃ ‘‘tosessāmī’’ti ekena pakkhena ñātiṃ karoti, khattiyo kirāhamasmī’’ti cintesi.
273-274.
Dakkhiṇajanapada means dakkhiṇāpatha, it is well-known.
South of the river Gaṅgā is the well-known janapada.
At that time, it seems there were many brāhmaṇa ascetics in the dakkhiṇāpatha. Having gone there, he pleased one ascetic with proper conduct and service.
Having seen his assistance, he said, "Hey, man, I will give you a mantra. Take whichever mantra you desire."
He said, "I have no need, teacher (ācariya), for another mantra. Give me that mantra by the power (anubhāva) of which a weapon does not turn away."
He said, "Good, sir," and he gave him the science (vijja) named ambaṭṭha, which could reach a bow. Having taken that science (vijja) and pondered it right there, thinking, "Now I will fulfill my wish (manoratha)," having taken the garb of a seer (iṣivesa), he went to Okkāka.
Thus it was said: "Having gone to dakkhiṇajanapada, having studied the Brahmanical mantras (brahmamante adhīyitvā), having approached King Okkāka (rājānaṃ okkākaṃ upasaṅkamitvā)."
Here, brahmamanta means the best mantra (seṭṭhamante) due to its power (ānubhāva) and accomplishment (sampannatāya).
Who would not say, 'Hey, this is the son of my female slave (dāsiputto)?' Who would say, 'Hey, this is my slave's son?'
"A sound, a razor": That king, wishing to kill him, prepared an arrow. By the power (mantānubhāvena) of his mantra, he was neither able to release it nor withdraw it. At once, sweat arose on his whole body, and trembling with fear, he stood there.
Amacca means great ministers (mahāmaccā).
Pārisajja means the other attendants (parisāvacarā).
Thus they said, "The entire kingdom was destroyed by a rain of weapons because Daṇḍakī, the king, offended Kisavaccha, the ascetic.
Nāḷikera offended five hundreds of ascetics, and Arjuna and Aṅgīrasa, having broken through the earth, entered hell," thinking this, out of fear, they spoke words of well-being (sotthi), words beginning with "Good sir (bhaddante)."
"Well-being (sotthi) will come to the king": Kanhha, having remained silent for a long time after this statement, being entreated in various ways, later spoke, "The king has done a weighty deed by aiming a razor at an ascetic like me," and other such things.
"It will be shattered (undriyissati)": it will be split apart, it will be scattered like a handful of chaff (thusamuṭṭhi).
This he speaks falsely, "I will frighten the people (janaṃ tāsessāmī)."
The power (ānubhāva) of his science (vijjāya) is only to stop the arrow (sarasanthambhana), not elsewhere.
The same method applies in the following statements as well (ito paresupi vacanesu eseva nayo).
Palloma means pannaloma.
He will not even have a hair's breadth of goosebumps (lomahaṃsanamattampissa na bhavissati).
It seems he spoke after making the promise, "If the king gives me that girl (dārikaṃ)."
"He established a razor on the boy (kumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhapesīti)" therefore, he repeated the mantras, "May the arrow descend (saro otaratū)," and he established them on the boy's navel (nābhiyaṃ patiṭṭhapesi).
"He gave a daughter (dhītaraṃ adāsīti)": Having washed her head and having made a slave a free person (bhujissaṃ katvā), he gave his daughter (dhītaraṃ adāsi), and he established her in a noble place (uḷāre ca taṃ ṭhāne ṭhapesi).
"Don't [say that], you youths (mā kho tumhe māṇavakāti)": Furthermore, the Blessed One (bhagavā), revealing, "By one side, Ambaṭṭha is a relative (ñāti hotī) of the Sakyas (sakyānaṃ)," spoke for his consolation (samassāsanatthaṃ āha).
Then, Ambaṭṭha, having become calmed and relieved, as if he had been anointed with a hundred pots, having consoled himself, thought, "The ascetic Gotama, wanting to please me (tosessāmī), makes a relative (ñātiṃ karoti) by one side. Surely, I am a warrior (khattiyo)!"
274
274. Atha kho bhagavato etadahosi – ‘‘atibāḷhaṃ kho ime māṇavakā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ dāsiputtavādena nimmādenti, yaṃnūnāhaṃ parimoceyya’’nti. Atha kho bhagavā te māṇavake etadavoca – ‘‘mā kho tumhe, māṇavakā, ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ atibāḷhaṃ dāsiputtavādena nimmādetha. Uḷāro so kaṇho isi ahosi. So dakkhiṇajanapadaṃ gantvā brahmamante adhīyitvā rājānaṃ okkākaṃ upasaṅkamitvā maddarūpiṃ dhītaraṃ yāci. Tassa rājā okkāko – ‘ko nevaṃ re ayaṃ mayhaṃ dāsiputto samāno maddarūpiṃ dhītaraṃ yācatī’’’ ti, kupito anattamano khurappaṃ sannayhi sannahi (ka.). So taṃ khurappaṃ neva asakkhi muñcituṃ, no paṭisaṃharituṃ. ‘‘Atha kho, māṇavakā, amaccā pārisajjā kaṇhaṃ isiṃ upasaṅkamitvā etadavocuṃ – ‘sotthi, bhaddante bhadante (sī. syā.), hotu rañño; sotthi, bhaddante, hotu rañño’ti. ‘Sotthi bhavissati rañño, api ca rājā yadi adho khurappaṃ muñcissati, yāvatā rañño vijitaṃ, ettāvatā pathavī undriyissatī’ti. ‘Sotthi, bhaddante, hotu rañño, sotthi janapadassā’ti. ‘Sotthi bhavissati rañño, sotthi janapadassa, api ca rājā yadi uddhaṃ khurappaṃ muñcissati, yāvatā rañño vijitaṃ, ettāvatā satta vassāni devo na vassissatī’ti. ‘Sotthi, bhaddante, hotu rañño sotthi janapadassa devo ca vassatū’ti. ‘Sotthi bhavissati rañño sotthi janapadassa devo ca vassissati, api ca rājā jeṭṭhakumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhāpetu, sotthi kumāro pallomo bhavissatī’ti. Atha kho, māṇavakā, amaccā okkākassa ārocesuṃ – ‘okkāko jeṭṭhakumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhāpetu. Sotthi kumāro pallomo bhavissatī’ti. Atha kho rājā okkāko jeṭṭhakumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhapesi, sotthi kumāro pallomo samabhavi. Atha kho tassa rājā okkāko bhīto saṃviggo lomahaṭṭhajāto brahmadaṇḍena tajjito maddarūpiṃ dhītaraṃ adāsi. Mā kho tumhe, māṇavakā, ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ atibāḷhaṃ dāsiputtavādena nimmādetha, uḷāro so kaṇho isi ahosī’’ti.
Then the Lord thought: "These young men are excessively humiliating Ambaṭṭha, the young man, by calling him the son of a slave girl. I wish I could free him from this."
Then the Lord said to the young men: "Young men, do not excessively humiliate Ambaṭṭha, the young man, by calling him the son of a slave girl.
That Kaṇha was a mighty sage ( uḷāro so kaṇho isi ahosi).
He went to the southern country, learned the brahmamantras (sacred Brahmin mantras) and approached King Okkāka, asking for his daughter Maddarūpī.
Then King Okkāka thought, "Who is this fellow, a slave-born, daring to ask for Maddarūpī as a wife?" Angered and displeased, he readied an arrow (khurappaṃ).
But he could neither release the arrow nor withdraw it.
Then the ministers and courtiers approached Kaṇha the sage and said: "May the king be safe, Reverend Sir, may the king be safe!"
"The king will be safe, but if the king releases the arrow downwards, the earth will quake as far as the king's dominion extends."
"May the king be safe, Reverend Sir, may the land be safe!"
"The king will be safe, the land will be safe, but if the king releases the arrow upwards, for seven years the god will not send rain as far as the king's dominion extends."
"May the king be safe, Reverend Sir, may the land be safe, and may the god send rain!"
"The king will be safe, the land will be safe, and the god will send rain, but if the king aims the arrow at the eldest prince, the prince will be perfectly safe."
Then the ministers announced to Okkāka: "Let Okkāka aim the arrow at the eldest prince.
The prince will be perfectly safe!"
Then King Okkāka aimed the arrow at the eldest prince, and the prince was perfectly safe.
Then King Okkāka, frightened, agitated, with his hair standing on end, intimidated by the brahmadaṇḍa (divine punishment), gave his daughter Maddarūpī.
"Young men, do not excessively humiliate Ambaṭṭha, the young man, by calling him the son of a slave girl. That Kaṇha was a mighty sage."
273-274.Dakkhiṇajanapadanti dakkhiṇāpathoti pākaṭaṃ. Gaṅgāya dakkhiṇato pākaṭajanapadaṃ. Tadā kira dakkhiṇāpathe bahū brāhmaṇatāpasā honti, so tattha gantvā ekaṃ tāpasaṃ vattapaṭipattiyā ārādhesi. So tassa upakāraṃ disvā āha – ‘‘ambho, purisa, mantaṃ te demi, yaṃ icchasi, taṃ mantaṃ gaṇhāhī’’ti. So āha – ‘‘na me ācariya, aññena mantena, kiccaṃ atthi, yassānubhāvena āvudhaṃ na parivattati, taṃ me mantaṃ dehī’’ti. So – ‘‘bhadraṃ, bho’’ti tassa dhanuagamanīyaṃ ambaṭṭhaṃ nāma vijjaṃ adāsi, so taṃ vijjaṃ gahetvā tattheva vīmaṃsitvā – ‘‘idāni me manorathaṃ pūressāmī’’ti isivesaṃ gahetvā okkākassa santikaṃ gato. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘dakkhiṇajanapadaṃ gantvā brahmamante adhīyitvā rājānaṃ okkākaṃ upasaṅkamitvā’’ti. Ettha brahmamanteti ānubhāvasampannatāya seṭṭhamante. Ko nevaṃ’re ayaṃ mayhaṃ dāsiputtoti ko nu evaṃ are ayaṃ mama dāsiputto. Sotaṃ khurappanti so rājā taṃ māretukāmatāya sannahitaṃ saraṃ tassa mantānubhāvena neva khipituṃ na apanetuṃ sakkhi, tāvadeva sakalasarīre sañjātasedo bhayena vedhamāno aṭṭhāsi. Amaccāti mahāmaccā. Pārisajjāti itare parisāvacarā. Etadavocunti – ‘‘daṇḍakīrañño kisavacchatāpase aparaddhassa āvudhavuṭṭhiyā sakalaraṭṭhaṃ vinaṭṭhaṃ . Nāḷikero pañcasu tāpasasatesu ajjuno ca aṅgīrase aparaddho pathaviṃ bhinditvā nirayaṃ paviṭṭho’’ti cintayantā bhayena etaṃ sotthi, bhaddantetiādivacanaṃ avocuṃ. Sotthi bhavissati raññoti idaṃ vacanaṃ kaṇho ciraṃ tuṇhī hutvā tato anekappakāraṃ yācīyamāno – ‘‘tumhākaṃ raññā mādisassa isino khurappaṃ sannayhantena bhāriyaṃ kammaṃ kata’’ntiādīni ca vatvā pacchā abhāsi. Undriyissatīti bhijjissati, thusamuṭṭhi viya vippakiriyissatīti. Idaṃ so ‘‘janaṃ tāsessāmī’’ti musā bhaṇati. Sarasanthambhanamatteyeva hissa vijjāya ānubhāvo, na aññatra. Ito paresupi vacanesu eseva nayo. Pallomoti pannalomo. Lomahaṃsanamattampissa na bhavissati. Idaṃ kira so ‘‘sace me rājā taṃ dārikaṃ dassatī’’ti paṭiññaṃ kāretvā avaca. Kumāre khurappaṃ patiṭṭhapesīti tena ‘‘saro otaratū’’ti mante parivatti, te kumārassa nābhiyaṃ patiṭṭhapesi. Dhītaraṃ adāsīti sīsaṃ dhovitvā adāsaṃ bhujissaṃ katvā dhītaraṃ adāsi, uḷāre ca taṃ ṭhāne ṭhapesi. Mā kho tumhe māṇavakāti idaṃ pana bhagavā – ‘‘ekena pakkhena ambaṭṭho sakyānaṃ ñāti hotī’’ti pakāsento tassa samassāsanatthaṃ āha. Tato ambaṭṭho ghaṭasatena abhisitto viya passaddhadaratho hutvā samassāsetvā samaṇo gotamo maṃ ‘‘tosessāmī’’ti ekena pakkhena ñātiṃ karoti, khattiyo kirāhamasmī’’ti cintesi.
273-274.

" Dakkhiṇajanapada " means dakkhiṇāpatha, [which is] well-known.

Pākaṭajanapada [is] to the south of the Ganges.

At that time, it seems, there were many brāhmaṇa ascetics in the Southern region (dakkhiṇāpatha); going there, he pleased an ascetic by attending to his duties.

Having seen his service, he said, "Hey, man, I will give you a mantra. Whatever mantra you wish, take that mantra."

He said, "I have no need, teacher (ācariya), for another mantra. Give me that mantra by the power (anubhāva) of which a weapon (āvudha) does not turn back."

He gave him the science (vijjā) named ambaṭṭha, which is attainable by bows, [saying], "Good, sir (bhadraṃ, bho)." Having taken that science (vijjā), and having pondered it there, [thinking,] "Now I will fulfill my wish (manorathaṃ pūressāmī)," having taken the dress of a hermit (isivesaṃ), he went to Okkāka's presence (santikaṃ).

Therefore it was said, "Having gone to the Southern region (dakkhiṇajanapadaṃ), having studied the Brahmanic mantras (brahmamante adhīyitvā), having approached King Okkāka (rājānaṃ okkākaṃ upasaṅkamitvā)."

Here, brahmamanta means the best mantras (seṭṭhamante) because of the perfection of [their] power (ānubhāva-sampannatāya).

"Who is it that doesn't [think like this]: 'Hey, is this the son of my slave woman?'" or "Who is it that thinks thus: 'Hey, is this my slave woman's son?'"

"A razor-edged ear (sotaṃ khurappaṃ)": that king, wishing to kill him (māretukāmatāya), was not able, by the power (anubhāva) of his mantra (mantānubhāvena), either to throw or remove the arrow (saraṃ sannahitaṃ); at that very moment, sweat arose on his whole body, and he stood trembling with fear (bhayena vedhamāno aṭṭhāsi).

Amacca means chief ministers (mahāmaccā).

Pārisajja means the other attendants (parisāvacarā).

They said, "Because Daṇḍakī, the king, offended Kisavaccha the ascetic (tāpase), the whole kingdom (sakala-raṭṭhaṃ) was destroyed (vinaṭṭhaṃ) by a shower of weapons (āvudha-vuṭṭhiyā)."

Thinking, "Nāḷikera, Arjuna, and Aṅgīrasa, having offended, fell into hell (nirayaṃ paviṭṭho) after splitting the earth (pathaviṃ bhinditvā)," they spoke this word (vacanaṃ): "Safety (sotthi)! Good sir (bhaddante)!" and other such things, out of fear (bhayena).

Kaṇha, being asked in many ways (anekappakāraṃ yācīyamāno) after being silent (tuṇhī hutvā) for a long time (ciraṃ), said this word (vacanaṃ): "Safety (sotthi) will be to the king (rañño)!" And after speaking, he said (abhāsi) afterwards, "Your king has done a weighty deed (bhāriyaṃ kammaṃ kata) by bringing a razor-edged ear (khurappaṃ sannayhantena) to an ascetic (isino) like me (mādisassa)."

"Undriyissati" means it will be broken (bhijjissati), "it will be scattered (vippakiriyissati) like a handful of chaff (thusamuṭṭhi viya)."

He says this falsely (musā bhaṇati), [thinking,] "I will terrify (tāsessāmī) the people (janaṃ)."

The power (ānubhāvo) of his science (vijjāya) is only in stopping the arrow (sarasanthambhana-matteyyeva), not elsewhere (aññatra).

In the following statements (ito paresupi vacanesu) also, this is the method (eseva nayo).

Palloma means pannaloma.

Even a hair's breadth (lomahaṃsanamattampissa) will not be.

It seems that he said this (avaca) after having made the promise (paṭiññaṃ kāretvā), [saying,] "If the king gives (dassatī) that girl (dārikaṃ) to me (me)."

Because he placed (patiṭṭhapesi) a razor-edged ear (khurappaṃ) on the boy (kumāre), therefore he turned (parivatti) the mantras (mante) [saying], "Let the arrow descend (saro otaratū)," he placed (patiṭṭhapesi) those [mantras] on the boy's navel (kumārassa nābhiyaṃ).

"He gave (adāsī) [his] daughter (dhītaraṃ)": having washed (dhovitvā) his head (sīsaṃ), he gave (adāsi) [his] daughter (dhītaraṃ), having made the slave (adāsaṃ) free (bhujissaṃ katvā), and he placed (ṭhapesi) her in a noble (uḷāre) place (ṭhāne).

"Don't [say], 'Hey, youths (mā kho tumhe māṇavakā)!'" The Blessed One (bhagavā), revealing (pakāsento) this, that "by one side, Ambaṭṭha is related (ñāti hotī) to the Sakyans (sakyānaṃ)," spoke (āha) for the sake of comforting (samassāsanatthaṃ) him (tassa).

Then, Ambaṭṭha, having become delighted (passaddhadaratho hutvā) as if sprinkled (abhisitto viya) with a hundred jars (ghaṭasatena), and having been consoled (samassāsetvā), thought (cintesi), "The ascetic (samaṇo) Gotama is making kinship (ñātiṃ karoti) by one side, [saying] 'I will please (tosessāmī) him (maṃ)'; surely (kira), I am a warrior (khattiyo asmi)."
275
275. Atha kho bhagavā ambaṭṭhaṃ māṇavaṃ āmantesi – ‘‘taṃ kiṃ maññasi, ambaṭṭha, idha khattiyakumāro brāhmaṇakaññāya saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ kappeyya, tesaṃ saṃvāsamanvāya putto jāyetha. Yo so khattiyakumārena brāhmaṇakaññāya putto uppanno, api nu so labhetha brāhmaṇesu āsanaṃ vā udakaṃ vā’’ti? ‘‘Labhetha, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ brāhmaṇā bhojeyyuṃ saddhe vā thālipāke vā yaññe vā pāhune vā’’ti? ‘‘Bhojeyyuṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ brāhmaṇā mante vāceyyuṃ vā no vā’’ti? ‘‘Vāceyyuṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinussa itthīsu āvaṭaṃ vā assa anāvaṭaṃ vā’’ti? ‘‘Anāvaṭaṃ hissa, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ khattiyā khattiyābhisekena abhisiñceyyu’’nti? ‘‘No hidaṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Taṃ kissa hetu’’? ‘‘Mātito hi, bho gotama, anupapanno’’ti. ‘‘Taṃ kiṃ maññasi, ambaṭṭha, idha brāhmaṇakumāro khattiyakaññāya saddhiṃ saṃvāsaṃ kappeyya, tesaṃ saṃvāsamanvāya putto jāyetha. Yo so brāhmaṇakumārena khattiyakaññāya putto uppanno, apinu so labhetha brāhmaṇesu āsanaṃ vā udakaṃ vā’’ti? ‘‘Labhetha, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ brāhmaṇā bhojeyyuṃ saddhe vā thālipāke vā yaññe vā pāhune vā’’ti? ‘‘Bhojeyyuṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ brāhmaṇā mante vāceyyuṃ vā no vā’’ti? ‘‘Vāceyyuṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinussa itthīsu āvaṭaṃ vā assa anāvaṭaṃ vā’’ti? ‘‘Anāvaṭaṃ hissa, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Apinu naṃ khattiyā khattiyābhisekena abhisiñceyyu’’nti? ‘‘No hidaṃ, bho gotama’’. ‘‘Taṃ kissa hetu’’? ‘‘Pitito hi, bho gotama, anupapanno’’ti.
Then the Lord addressed the young Ambaṭṭha: "What do you think, Ambaṭṭha? If a khattiya youth were to cohabit with a brāhmaṇa maiden, and a son were born to them as a result of that cohabitation, and should that son of a khattiya youth and a brāhmaṇa maiden receive a seat or water from the brāhmaṇas?" "He would receive it, Master Gotama."

"And would the brāhmaṇas feed him at a saddha (funeral rite), or at a thālipāka (rice-milk offering), or at a yañña (sacrifice), or as a guest?" "They would feed him, Master Gotama."

"And would the brāhmaṇas recite mantras to him, or not?" "They would recite them, Master Gotama."

"And would their women be covered or uncovered to him?" "Uncovered, sir, Master Gotama."

"And would the khattiyas anoint him with the khattiya anointment?" "No, that they would not, Master Gotama."

"What is the reason for that?" "Because he is not born of the mother’s side, sir, Master Gotama."

"What do you think, Ambaṭṭha? If a brāhmaṇa youth were to cohabit with a khattiya maiden, and a son were born to them as a result of that cohabitation, and should that son of a brāhmaṇa youth and a khattiya maiden receive a seat or water from the brāhmaṇas?" "He would receive it, Master Gotama."

"And would the brāhmaṇas feed him at a saddha (funeral rite), or at a thālipāka (rice-milk offering), or at a yañña (sacrifice), or as a guest?" "They would feed him, Master Gotama."

"And would the brāhmaṇas recite mantras to him, or not?" "They would recite them, Master Gotama."

"And would their women be covered or uncovered to him?" "Uncovered, sir, Master Gotama."

"And would the khattiyas anoint him with the khattiya anointment?" "No, that they would not, Master Gotama."

"What is the reason for that?" "Because he is not born of the father’s side, sir, Master Gotama."
275. Atha kho bhagavā – ‘‘ayaṃ ambaṭṭho khattiyosmī’’ti saññaṃ karoti, attano akhattiyabhāvaṃ na jānāti, handa naṃ jānāpessāmīti khattiyavaṃsaṃ dassetuṃ uttaridesanaṃ vaḍḍhento – ‘‘taṃ kiṃ maññasi ambaṭṭhā’’tiādimāha. Tattha idhāti imasmiṃ loke. Brāhmaṇesūti brāhmaṇānaṃ antare. Āsanaṃ vā udakaṃ vāti aggāsanaṃ vā aggodakaṃ vā. Saddheti matake uddissa katabhatte. Thālipāketi maṅgalādibhatte. Yaññeti yaññabhatte. Pāhuneti pāhunakānaṃ katabhatte paṇṇākārabhatte vā. Api nussāti api nu assa khattiyaputtassa. Āvaṭaṃ vā assa anāvaṭaṃ vāti , brāhmaṇakaññāsu nivāraṇaṃ bhaveyya vā no vā, brāhmaṇadārikaṃ labheyya vā na vā labheyyāti attho. Anupapannoti khattiyabhāvaṃ apatto, aparisuddhoti attho.
