Then the brahmin Pokkharasāti examined the Blessed One’s body for the thirty-two marks of a great man (dvātriṃśatmahāpuruṣalakṣaṇāni).

And the brahmin Pokkharasāti saw nearly all the thirty-two marks of a great man on the Blessed One's body, except for two.

He was doubtful and perplexed about two of these marks and could not decide or be certain about them: about the male organ being enclosed in a sheath (kosohite ca vatthaguyhe) and about the largeness of the tongue (pahūtajivhatāya ca).
292-296.Ativikāloti suṭṭhu vikālo, sammodanīyakathāyapi kālo natthi. Āgamā nu khvidha bhoti āgamā nu kho idha bho. Adhivāsetūti sampaṭicchatu. Ajjatanāyāti yaṃ me tumhesu kāraṃ karoto ajja bhavissati puññañca pītipāmojjañca tadatthāya. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvenāti bhagavā kāyaṅgaṃ vā vācaṅgaṃ vā acopetvā abbhantareyeva khantiṃ dhārento tuṇhībhāvena adhivāsesi. Brāhmaṇassa anuggahaṇatthaṃ manasāva sampaṭicchīti vuttaṃ hoti.
Very late means exceedingly late; there is no time even for polite conversation.

Do the Āgamas exist here? Are there Āgamas here, sir?

Let him accept, meaning: may he consent.

'For today' means: whatever merit (puṇya) and joy-and-gladness will arise today for me, while I am doing an act of worship for you, is for that purpose.

'The Exalted One consented in silence' means: the Exalted One, without stirring either his bodily action (kāyaṅga) or his speech-action (vācaṅga), enduring patience within himself, consented in silence.

'He accepted it in his mind for the sake of favouring the brāhmaṇa'—this is what is said.
295
295. Atha kho bhagavato etadahosi – ‘‘passati kho me ayaṃ brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti dvattiṃsamahāpurisalakkhaṇāni yebhuyyena ṭhapetvā dve. Dvīsu mahāpurisalakkhaṇesu kaṅkhati vicikicchati nādhimuccati na sampasīdati – kosohite ca vatthaguyhe, pahūtajivhatāya cā’’ti. Atha kho bhagavā tathārūpaṃ iddhābhisaṅkhāraṃ abhisaṅkhāsi yathā addasa brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti bhagavato kosohitaṃ vatthaguyhaṃ. Atha kho bhagavā jivhaṃ ninnāmetvā ubhopi kaṇṇasotāni anumasi paṭimasi, ubhopi nāsikasotāni anumasi paṭimasi, kevalampi nalāṭamaṇḍalaṃ jivhāya chādesi.
295. Then it occurred to the Blessed One: "This Brahmin Pokkharasāti sees in me the thirty-two marks of a Great Man, for the most part, except for two.

He doubts and is uncertain about two of the marks of a Great Man, he cannot decide and is not confident about them: about the male organ being enclosed in a sheath (kosohite vatthaguyhe), and about the largeness of the tongue (pahūtajivhatāya)."

Then the Blessed One worked such a feat of supernormal power that the Brahmin Pokkharasāti saw that the Blessed One’s male organ was enclosed in a sheath (kosohitaṃ vatthaguyhaṃ).

Next the Blessed One extended his tongue, and he repeatedly touched both ear holes and both nostrils, and he covered the whole circle of his forehead with his tongue.
292-296.Ativikāloti suṭṭhu vikālo, sammodanīyakathāyapi kālo natthi. Āgamā nu khvidha bhoti āgamā nu kho idha bho. Adhivāsetūti sampaṭicchatu. Ajjatanāyāti yaṃ me tumhesu kāraṃ karoto ajja bhavissati puññañca pītipāmojjañca tadatthāya. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvenāti bhagavā kāyaṅgaṃ vā vācaṅgaṃ vā acopetvā abbhantareyeva khantiṃ dhārento tuṇhībhāvena adhivāsesi. Brāhmaṇassa anuggahaṇatthaṃ manasāva sampaṭicchīti vuttaṃ hoti.
Very late is indeed quite late; there is no time even for words of greeting.

Are the āgamas coming here? Are the āgamas coming here, sir?

Let him accept it, meaning let him receive it well.

For today, whatever merit, joy, and delight will be mine through performing the act of reverence toward you.

The Exalted One accepted with silence, meaning the Exalted One, without stirring bodily or vocal action, enduring patience within himself, accepted with silence.

It is said that he accepted it in his mind for the sake of favoring the brāhmaṇa.
296
296. Atha kho brāhmaṇassa pokkharasātissa etadahosi – ‘‘samannāgato kho samaṇo gotamo dvattiṃsamahāpurisalakkhaṇehi paripuṇṇehi no aparipuṇṇehī’’ti. Bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘adhivāsetu me bhavaṃ gotamo ajjatanāya bhattaṃ saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghenā’’ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.
Then the brahmin Pokkharasāti thought: “The ascetic Gotama is endowed with the thirty-two marks of a great man (dvātriṃśat-mahāpuruṣa-lakṣaṇa), complete and not incomplete.”

He said to the Blessed One: “May the venerable Gotama accept a meal from me today, together with the bhikkhu-saṅgha (community of monks).”

The Blessed One consented by silence (tuṇhībhāvena).
292-296.Ativikāloti suṭṭhu vikālo, sammodanīyakathāyapi kālo natthi. Āgamā nu khvidha bhoti āgamā nu kho idha bho. Adhivāsetūti sampaṭicchatu. Ajjatanāyāti yaṃ me tumhesu kāraṃ karoto ajja bhavissati puññañca pītipāmojjañca tadatthāya. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvenāti bhagavā kāyaṅgaṃ vā vācaṅgaṃ vā acopetvā abbhantareyeva khantiṃ dhārento tuṇhībhāvena adhivāsesi. Brāhmaṇassa anuggahaṇatthaṃ manasāva sampaṭicchīti vuttaṃ hoti.
292-296.
Ativikāla means very much vikāla; there is no time even for pleasant conversation.
Are there āgamas here? Are there āgamas here, sir?
Let him accept, saying, "May he consent."
For today, whatever merit, joy, and delight will be mine as I perform acts of kindness for you today, is for that purpose.
The Exalted One consented in silence. The Exalted One, without arousing bodily or verbal action, enduring patience inwardly, consented in silence.
It is said that he accepted it in his mind for the sake of favoring the brāhmaṇa.
297
297. Atha kho brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā bhagavato kālaṃ ārocesi – ‘‘kālo, bho gotama, niṭṭhitaṃ bhatta’’nti. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena brāhmaṇassa pokkharasātissa nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Atha kho brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti bhagavantaṃ paṇītena khādanīyena bhojanīyena sahatthā santappesi sampavāresi, māṇavakāpi bhikkhusaṅghaṃ. Atha kho brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti bhagavantaṃ bhuttāviṃ onītapattapāṇiṃ aññataraṃ nīcaṃ āsanaṃ gahetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi.
Then, the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti, knowing the Blessed One's acceptance, announced the time to the Blessed One, saying: “It is time, Reverend Gotama, the meal is ready.”

Then, the Blessed One, having dressed in the morning, taking his bowl (patta) and robe (cīvara), together with the community of monks (bhikkhusaṅgha), went to the residence (nivesanaṃ) of the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti; having arrived, he sat down on the prepared seat (āsana).

Then, the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti personally satisfied and served the Blessed One with choice staple and non-staple food (khādanīya bhojanīya), and the young men also served the community of monks (bhikkhusaṅgha).

Then, the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti, when the Blessed One had finished eating and removed his hand from the bowl, took a low seat (nīcaṃ āsanaṃ) and sat down to one side.
297.Paṇītenāti uttamena. Sahatthāti sahatthena. Santappesīti suṭṭhu tappesi paripuṇṇaṃ suhitaṃ yāvadatthaṃ akāsi. Sampavāresīti suṭṭhu pavāresi, alaṃ alanti hatthasaññāya paṭikkhipāpesi. Bhuttāvinti bhuttavantaṃ. Onītapattapāṇinti pattato onītapāṇiṃ, apanītahatthanti vuttaṃ hoti. Onittapattapāṇintipi pāṭho. Tassattho – onittaṃ nānābhūtaṃ vinābhūtaṃ pattaṃ pāṇito assāti onittapattapāṇi, taṃ onittapattapāṇiṃ. Hatthe ca pattañca dhovitvā ekamante pattaṃ nikkhipitvā nisinnanti attho. Ekamantaṃ nisīdīti bhagavantaṃ evaṃ bhūtaṃ ñatvā ekasmiṃ okāse nisīdīti attho.
297.
paṇītena means with the best or excellent (uttamena).
sahatthā means with his own hand (sahatthena).
santappesi means he satisfied him well, he made him full, happy, and to his heart's content (suṭṭhu tappesi paripuṇṇaṃ suhitaṃ yāvadatthaṃ akāsi).
sampavāresi means he forbade him well, he rejected him with a gesture of the hand, saying "Enough! Enough!" (suṭṭhu pavāresi, alaṃ alanti hatthasaññāya paṭikkhipāpesi).
bhuttāviṃ means one who has eaten (bhuttavantaṃ).
onītapattapāṇiṃ means one who has removed his hand from the bowl, it is said that he has removed his hand (pattato onītapāṇiṃ, apanītahatthanti vuttaṃ hoti).
There is also the reading onittapattapāṇiṃ.
Its meaning is: onittaṃ means different, separate, the bowl is without the hand, therefore onittapattapāṇi, him the onittapattapāṇiṃ.
The meaning is: Having washed his hands and the bowl, and having placed the bowl aside, he sat down (hatthe ca pattañca dhovitvā ekamante pattaṃ nikkhipitvā nisinnanti attho).
ekamantaṃ nisīdīti means, having known the Blessed One to be thus, he sat down in a certain place (bhagavantaṃ evaṃ bhūtaṃ ñatvā ekasmiṃ okāse nisīdīti attho).
298
298. Ekamantaṃ nisinnassa kho brāhmaṇassa pokkharasātissa bhagavā anupubbiṃ kathaṃ kathesi, seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ; kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ, nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā bhagavā aññāsi brāhmaṇaṃ pokkharasātiṃ kallacittaṃ muducittaṃ vinīvaraṇacittaṃ udaggacittaṃ pasannacittaṃ, atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā, taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ samudayaṃ nirodhaṃ maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya; evameva brāhmaṇassa pokkharasātissa tasmiññeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’’nti.
And as the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti was seated to one side, the Blessed One delivered a graduated discourse, that is to say, a talk on generosity (dānakathā), a talk on morality (sīlakathā), a talk on heaven (saggakathā), showing the danger (ādīnava), degradation (okāra), and corruption (saṃkilesa) of sense-desires, and the profit (ānisaṃsa) of renunciation (nekkhamma).

When the Blessed One knew that the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti’s mind was ready (kallacittaṃ), pliable (muducittaṃ), free from hindrances (vinīvaraṇacittaṃ), joyful (udaggacittaṃ), and calm (pasannacittaṃ), then he preached the teaching special to the Buddhas (buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā)— suffering (dukkhaṃ), its origin (samudayaṃ), its cessation (nirodhaṃ), and the path (maggaṃ).

Just as a clean cloth, free from stains (apagatakāḷakaṃ), would readily take dye, so too, as the brāhmaṇa Pokkharasāti sat in that very seat, there arose in him the spotless, immaculate eye of the Dharma (virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ): “Whatever is subject to arising (samudayadhammaṃ) is all subject to cessation (nirodhadhammaṃ).”
298.Anupubbiṃ kathanti anupaṭipāṭikathaṃ. Ānupubbikathā nāma dānānantaraṃ sīlaṃ, sīlānantaraṃ saggo, saggānantaraṃ maggoti etesaṃ atthānaṃ dīpanakathā. Teneva – ‘‘seyyathidaṃ dānakatha’’ntiādimāha. Okāranti avakāraṃ lāmakabhāvaṃ. Sāmukkaṃsikāti sāmaṃ ukkaṃsikā, attanāyeva uddharitvā gahitā, sayambhūñāṇena diṭṭhā, asādhāraṇā aññesanti attho. Kā pana sāti? Ariyasaccadesanā. Tenevāha – ‘‘dukkhaṃ, samudayaṃ, nirodhaṃ, magga’’nti. Dhammacakkhunti ettha sotāpattimaggo adhippeto. Tassa uppattiākāradassanatthaṃ – ‘‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’’nti āha. Tañhi nirodhaṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā kiccavasena evaṃ sabbasaṅkhataṃ paṭivijjhantaṃ uppajjati.
298.

"Speaking gradually" means speaking sequentially.

The gradual teaching is so called because it is a talk that clarifies these meanings: giving (dāna) followed by morality (śīla), morality (śīla) followed by heaven (svarga), and heaven (svarga) followed by the path (mārga).

Therefore, he said, "For example, a talk on giving (dānakatha)," and so on.

"Okāra" means contempt, a base state.

"Sāmukkaṃsikā" means "one who raises oneself up," taken up by oneself, seen by self-born wisdom ( sayambhūñāṇa), not common to others, is the meaning.

What is that called "sāti"? It is the teaching of the noble truths (ariyasaccadesanā).

Therefore, he said, "Suffering (duḥkha), origination (samudaya), cessation (nirodha), path (mārga)."

"Dhammacakkhu" here means the path of stream-entry (sotāpattimagga) is intended.

To show its manner of arising, he said, "Whatever is of the nature of arising, all that is of the nature of ceasing."

For that arises thus, making cessation (nirodha) its object (ārammaṇa), penetrating all conditioned things (sarvasaṅkhata) according to its function.
299
299. Atha kho brāhmaṇo pokkharasāti diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo pariyogāḷhadhammo tiṇṇavicikiccho vigatakathaṃkatho vesārajjappatto aparappaccayo satthusāsane bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘abhikkantaṃ, bho gotama, abhikkantaṃ, bho gotama. Seyyathāpi, bho gotama, nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya , andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya, ‘cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantī’ti; evamevaṃ bhotā gotamena anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Esāhaṃ, bho gotama, saputto sabhariyo sapariso sāmacco bhavantaṃ gotamaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Upāsakaṃ maṃ bhavaṃ gotamo dhāretu ajjatagge pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gataṃ. Yathā ca bhavaṃ gotamo ukkaṭṭhāya aññāni upāsakakulāni upasaṅkamati, evameva bhavaṃ gotamo pokkharasātikulaṃ upasaṅkamatu. Tattha ye te māṇavakā vā māṇavikā vā bhavantaṃ gotamaṃ abhivādessanti vā paccuṭṭhissanti paccuṭṭhassanti (pī.) vā āsanaṃ vā udakaṃ vā dassanti cittaṃ vā pasādessanti, tesaṃ taṃ bhavissati dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā’’ti. ‘‘Kalyāṇaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇā’’ti.
Then the Brahmin Pokkharasāti, having seen the dharma, attained the dharma, understood the dharma, fathomed the dharma, having crossed beyond doubt, transcended uncertainty, having gained perfect confidence in the Teacher’s doctrine without relying on others, said this to the Blessed One: “Excellent, venerable Gotama, excellent, venerable Gotama!

Just as, venerable Gotama, one might set upright what has been overturned, or reveal what has been hidden, or point out the way to one who is lost, or hold up an oil lamp in the darkness so that those with eyes might see forms, in the same way the dharma has been made clear by venerable Gotama in many ways.

I, along with my son, my wife, my retinue, and my ministers, go to venerable Gotama for refuge, to the dharma, and to the monastic community (bhikkhusaṅgha).

May venerable Gotama remember me as a lay follower (upāsaka) who has gone for refuge from this day forth, as long as life shall last (pāṇupetaṃ saraṇaṃ gataṃ).

And just as venerable Gotama visits other families of lay followers (upāsakakulāni) in Ukkaṭṭha, may venerable Gotama also visit the family of Pokkharasāti!

And whatever young men or maidens there are who will greet venerable Gotama or rise up to meet him, or offer a seat or water, or gladden their hearts, that will be for their long-term welfare and happiness."

"Well said, Brahmin!" (kalyāṇaṃ vuccati, brāhmaṇā).
299. Diṭṭho ariyasaccadhammo etenāti diṭṭhadhammo. Esa nayo sesapadesupi. Tiṇṇā vicikicchā anenāti tiṇṇavicikiccho. Vigatā kathaṃkathā assāti vigatakathaṃkatho. Vesārajjappattoti visāradabhāvaṃ patto. Kattha? Satthusāsane. Nāssa paro paccayo, na parassa saddhāya ettha vattatīti aparappaccayo. Sesaṃ sabbattha vuttanayattā uttānatthattā ca pākaṭamevāti.
299. Because the noble truth of the Dharma has been seen by this, it is called diṭṭhadhamma.

The same method of explanation applies to the other clauses too.

Because doubt (vicikicchā) has been overcome by this, it is called tiṇṇavicikiccho.

Because questioning (kathaṃkathā) has vanished from him, he is called vigatakathaṃkatho.

He has attained confidence (vesārajja), therefore he has attained the state of confidence (visāradabhāvaṃ patto).

Where? In the Teacher's Dispensation (satthusāsane).

There is no other condition for him, nor does it proceed here by the faith of another, therefore it is independent of others (aparappaccayo).

The rest is clear everywhere because of the method already stated and because the meaning is plain.
